## Acceptance on a second machine · 2026-09-06 — backlog #19, machine 2 of 2: Windows 10 Pro (zh-TW system, en-US display), VMware

**Machine.** `DESKTOP-CO7QIMR`, a VMware Workstation guest (`VMware20,1`) with one e1000e adapter (`Intel(R) 82574L Gigabit Network Connection`, `HardwareInterface=True`, classified Physical by the shipped rule) on NAT, no wireless adapter (`WlanSvc` stopped, `netsh wlan` prints no SSID line, so the zero-`wifi`-row rule applies again). Microsoft Windows 10 專業版 22H2, build 19045.3803, EditionID `Professional`: `gpedit.msc` and `secpol.msc` both present, unlike machine 1. Windows PowerShell 5.1.19041.3803, .NET 4.8.04084, no `pwsh`. Display 1668 × 999 at 96 DPI. WDAC off, no AppLocker rule collections, `AppIDSvc` Stopped (Manual), Defender on, UAC on. The campaign ran unelevated as `test` (an administrator account, not elevated); `A2` ran as `nhc-test`, whose token holds no Administrators group at all.

**Why this machine.** It is the mirror of machine 1 in the two ways that matter. Machine 1 was a Windows 11 **Home** with an **en-GB** system running the zh-TW package; this is a Windows 10 **Pro** whose system locale is **zh-TW** (active code page 950) while its display language is **en-US**. Between them, backlog #19's "a system whose locale differs from the report language in both directions" is now measured in both directions, on both supported Windows versions, and on an edition that has the policy consoles as well as one that has none.

**Method.** The guided campaign `win10-zhTW` of `tests/Invoke-AcceptanceCampaign.ps1` on the merge run's asset — `NetworkHealthCheck-1.2.3.zip`, 262 375 bytes, SHA256 `b18bb699…`, the CI artifact of run 49 on `main` (commit `2a2a17b`), digest matched by the runner before anything else. Started 07:41, finished 09:25.

**Result: 10 of 11 scenarios PASS; `M9` FAIL — the machine did not enforce a policy it reported as enforcing, which is about the machine and not about the tool (below).**

| Scenario | Result | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| A1 baseline, the acceptance runner as it is | PASS | 31 cases, 0 failed, 482 s — including the new `launcher` step at 114 / 114, its first run away from the author's machine |
| A2 the same runner as a standard user (`nhc-test`) | PASS | 31 cases, 0 failed, 488 s; the probe confirms no Administrators group in the token |
| A3 host-only (an address, no gateway) | PASS | 10 cases + 1 skipped; **the `routes` row is `INFO — No IPv4 default route exists.` / `沒有 IPv4 預設路由。`** in all five reports of the scenario |
| A4 adapter disconnected | PASS | 10 cases + 1 skipped |
| M1 run from inside the compressed-folder view | PASS | the launcher's own stop, as stock Windows makes it: only the clicked file was extracted, the view held 2 files, no report. The Windows 10 view shape (`Temp1_…zip\`) |
| M2 extract without Unblock, double-click the root launcher | PASS | download mark `ZoneId=3`, extracted launcher mark `ZoneId=3`, Windows showed 1 warning, a report was written |
| M3 Unblock, extract, both root launchers, Open Report | PASS | no mark after Unblock; two reports; Open Report shown |
| M4 the IT window at 1366 × 768 | PASS | window 940 × 688, the panel usable |
| M7 every new PowerShell in ConstrainedLanguage | PASS | launcher exit 1, one environment report, no report |
| M8 Group Policy execution policy `AllSigned` | PASS | launcher exit 1, the signature-specific refusal (`is not digitally signed`) through `gpedit.msc` — the GPO path, which machine 1's Home edition could not take |
| M9 AppLocker script rules enforced | **FAIL** | the script ran unrestricted and wrote a report although the effective policy denied it — unexplained, below |

**What `M9` actually measured, and what remains unexplained.** The precondition was met on its own terms: Script rules `Enabled`, two rules (the default rule for `BUILTIN\Administrators` deleted so the account under test is not exempt), `AppIDSvc` running, `gpupdate /force` run, and `Test-AppLockerPolicy` answering `DeniedByDefault` for `DESKTOP-CO7QIMR\test` on the very file the launcher would run. The launcher then ran it: exit 0, no environment report, one report written, in `FullLanguage` — so neither AppLocker's enforcement nor its audit-mode constraint touched the run. **The tool did the right thing: nothing blocked it, so it ran.** Why nothing blocked it is not settled by this campaign. The obvious explanation — that Pro configures AppLocker without enforcing it — is *wrong for this build*: Microsoft's "Requirements to use AppLocker" states that "as of [KB 5024351](https://support.microsoft.com/help/5024351), Windows 10 versions 2004 and newer and all Windows 11 versions no longer require a specific edition of Windows to enforce AppLocker policies", and this machine is 22H2, build 19045.3803. So the honest record is: **AppLocker reported a policy that denied the script, and the machine ran it anyway; the cause is the machine or its servicing state, not the edition, and it is unresolved.** The campaign's `M9` therefore still runs on every edition that has AppLocker at all and every build at or above that floor — below it, a Group-Policy deployment, which is all `secpol.msc` can give, still needs Enterprise, Education or Server, and the prerequisite says so rather than spending the operator's afternoon — and its FAIL detail now names what to investigate instead of implying an edition rule. *(This paragraph replaces an earlier reading of the same evidence, which asserted the edition limitation and was corrected in review — the documentation above is what settled it.)*

**Two things the revert taught.** First, `sc config AppIDSvc start= demand` answered `Access is denied` after the Script rules were removed, although the same command had been accepted while they were in force; `net stop AppIDSvc` still worked. The service's own registry value (`HKLM\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Services\AppIDSvc\Start`, 3 = Manual) is what restored the startup type, and without it the machine would have kept an `Automatic` service it did not have before. `RECOVER.txt` and the scenario's revert instruction now carry that line. Second, the revert instruction spelled its command with a placeholder, `sc config AppIDSvc start= <auto|demand|disabled>`, and the placeholder was typed literally — the instruction now points at `RECOVER.txt`, which has the values already filled in, instead of showing a form to complete.

**One deviation from the path a user takes, recorded because it matters.** The asset was the CI artifact, and the artifact ZIP was not downloaded inside this VM, so neither it nor the package ZIP inside it carried a Mark of the Web. `M2` was skipped for the missing prerequisite on the first pass, and the mark was then applied by hand (`Zone.Identifier` with `ZoneId=3`, what a browser writes) and `M2` redone, where it passed. Windows treats a hand-written mark exactly as a downloaded one, and `M2`'s measurement — the warning dialog and the run that follows it — is therefore sound; but the download itself was not exercised on this machine, as it was on machine 1.

**Gaps of #19 closed by this machine.** Windows 10, which the tool claims to support and had never been run on; a system locale opposite to the report language in the second direction (zh-TW system, en-US display); an edition with both policy consoles, so `M8` was measured through a real Group Policy rather than through the registry; and a standard-user session on Windows 10. **Backlog #19 is closed: both machines are done.** What neither machine gave is AppLocker enforcement actually taking effect: Home has no AppLocker at all, and this Pro machine reported an enforcing policy that it then did not act on. `M9` stays unmeasured, and why is the open question its FAIL detail now names.

## v1.2.3 — what the first campaign found in the tool · 2026-09-06 — backlog #26, #27, #28

**Where the three come from.** The first campaign of backlog #19 (the three entries below: a Windows 11 Home VM, `win11-enUS`) accepted 1.2.2 and left three findings about the shipped package rather than about the driver, recorded as backlog #26, #27 and #28 on 2026-09-05. This release closes them and changes nothing else in the tool.

**#26 — the Windows 11 view folder.** `Test-IsRunningFromArchive` matched `\.zip[\\/]`: the Windows 10 view, `%TEMP%\Temp1_<name>.zip\`. Windows 11 extracts into `%TEMP%\<guid>_<name>.zip.<hex>\`, and the suffix is hexadecimal — `.684`, `.bc4`, `.515` on the VM, the last three digits of the GUID — so the item's own proposal, `\.zip(\.\d+)?`, would have missed two of the three folders the campaign met. The pattern is `\.zip(\.[0-9a-f]+)?[\\/]` now (`-match` is case-insensitive). The unit tests gained five cases for the shape: the two real folder names, forward slashes, the view folder itself rather than a folder inside it, and a backup folder (`pack.zip.old\`) that must not match. `env_guard_check.ps1` runs its compressed-folder scenario in both shapes, 13 → 18 cases per language, and one of its assertions is repaired on the way: "the reported path is not inside the view" required `.zip\` to follow the report's file name, which no output line can satisfy, so it could never fail; it now fails on any line that names both the report and a view folder. Stock Windows never reaches this code — Explorer extracts only the file clicked and the launcher stops first, checklist `M1` — so nothing a user does changes; an archiver that extracts the whole folder into the view, or a `.ps1` double-clicked there, does.

**#27 — a machine without a default route, and the tool's own timeouts.** In Windows PowerShell a CIM query that matches nothing is not an empty result. Measured on the reference machine: `Get-NetRoute -AddressFamily IPv4 -DestinationPrefix <a prefix that matches nothing> -ErrorAction Stop` throws `Microsoft.PowerShell.Cmdletization.Cim.CimJobException`, category `ObjectNotFound`, `FullyQualifiedErrorId` `CmdletizationQuery_NotFound,Get-NetRoute` — and its message is in the machine's display language (Chinese here: 「找不到相符的 MSFT_NetRoute 物件」), which is why the message is not the discriminator. The `catch` now takes that id as the empty set, so the `INFO — No IPv4 default route exists.` row is reachable at last, and every other exception still writes the `ERROR` row. `-ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` was the obvious alternative and was not taken: it would report a broken CIM server as "no default route". The other half: `Invoke-DnsLookup` and `Invoke-TcpConnectionTest` threw a string, which PowerShell wraps in a `RuntimeException`, so the details read `Error type: System.Management.Automation.RuntimeException` and carried no `Cause:` line. They throw a `System.TimeoutException` now, and `Get-NetworkErrorCauseText` classifies it as `[ToolTimeout]` with a sentence for what the situation is — nothing replied and nothing refused within the tool's own limit, the shape of a firewall dropping packets or an unreachable host — above the original message, like every other cause. The chain's unreachable TCP target, `192.0.2.1:9`, is exactly that on the reference machine (3025 ms, never signalled), so the acceptance step exercises the new path on every run, and its cause assertion counts `[ToolTimeout]` alongside the socket and web codes. Unit tests: the cause and the details for a `TimeoutException`, direct and wrapped; and because a real timeout needs a network that drops packets, the AST of both functions is checked for exactly one `throw` of `New-Object System.TimeoutException`.

**#28 — the launcher's suggested action.** All six language launchers (user, IT, console × two languages) set a `SUGGESTED` text beside `FAIL_REASON` at each stop: the program file missing → extract the complete ZIP, the one case where that advice was ever right; no PowerShell → ask IT; exit code 3, the code the script uses only for the language-mode guard → read `NetworkHealthCheck_ENVIRONMENT_*.txt` in this folder or in the temporary folder and send it; any other exit code → read the message PowerShell printed above the error and send it with the file, and if it names a signature or a policy, ask IT to allow `NetworkHealthCheck.ps1`. Exit code 3 gets its own `Error:` line saying what the code means (it still contains `exit code 3`, which the campaign's `M7` asserts). The line goes into `LauncherError.txt`, into the `%TEMP%` fallback file and onto the screen under the reason. The texts contain none of `( ) < > & |`: inside a parenthesised `cmd` block, a `)` in an expanded variable closes the block. The chain gained a `launcher` step — `tests/launcher_check.ps1`, also in `Invoke-PackageAcceptance.ps1`'s steps and on CI — that starts every launcher through every reason from a staged copy whose program file is missing or is a stub that exits 3 or 1, like a double-click with stdin from NUL: 18 runs, 114 assertions on the exit code, the file, its single suggested-action line (read on its own, because the `Script:` line names `NetworkHealthCheck.ps1` in every file; it must fit the reason and must not be another reason's), the `Error:` line of exit 3, and the screen. Against the 1.2.2 launchers it fails 42 of those 114 — every exit-3 and exit-1 run suggested the ZIP, and the screen never showed a suggestion — so it measures the change rather than the file's existence.

**Version.** 1.2.3 in both scripts, the packaged validator (`TOOL_VERSION`; `FUNCTION_COUNT` stays 81, no function was added), `README_BILINGUAL.md`, both user READMEs, both guides in all three locations and the repository README; `SHA256SUMS.txt` regenerated over the same 22 files.

**Chain from the branch (Windows 11 10.0.26200, Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100) — `-Steps parse,validator,guards,unit,report,envguard`: 10 cases, 0 failed, 27 s; `-Steps launcher`: 1 / 1, 4 s.** Parser 0 × 2; validator 62 / 62; guard self-test OK; unit **178 × 2** (163 before); report-stage 103 × 2; envguard **18 × 2** (13 before); launcher **114 / 114**.

**Release validation — `Invoke-ValidationChain.ps1 -Package -RequireHealthy` on commit `9468865`: 31 cases, 29 passed, 2 failed, 633 s; the two steps that failed were run again twice, 7 / 2 and then 9 / 0.** The full chain including the real-window steps, as the 1.2.0 rule requires when both shipped scripts change. Parser 0 × 2; validator 62 / 62; guard self-test OK; unit 178 × 2; report-stage 103 × 2; envguard 18 × 2; launcher 114 / 114; campaign self-test 123 / 123; headless GUI 4 / 4; real window 2 / 4; acceptance 4 / 5; result-set self-check 41 / 41; package: `NetworkHealthCheck-1.2.3.zip`, 261 322 bytes, SHA256 `0cbc1224…`, the validator 62 / 62 inside the extracted package, and its four real windows — the root launchers of both languages, the IT launchers of both — all Overall Healthy, titles carrying 1.2.3. The two failures were verdicts, not the code: the en-US IT window (30 pings / 125 s) ended Problem Detected with fingerprint `quality` on `TCPv4: sent 5087, retransmitted 122 (2.398 %)` — 122 is above the critical count of 50 — and the en-US user console run ended Attention Required on `1.1.1.1: 25 % loss (3/4)`. A rerun of `gui,acceptance` (395 s) failed two different cases the same way: the en-US user window on the gateway ping (`192.168.1.1: 25 % loss (3/4)`, fingerprint `gateway-unreachable`) and the en-US IT window again on `TCPv4: sent 4325, retransmitted 103 (2.382 %)` — while the zh-TW IT window at the same configuration, run right after it each time, saw 770 and 822 segments with 2 and 0 retransmissions. The difference was the host's own traffic during the sample: this session was working — commands, streamed output — over a Wi-Fi at 81 %, −63 dBm during the en-US IT windows and idle during the zh-TW ones. A third run of the same two steps with the session idle throughout: **9 / 9, 399 s**, every verdict Healthy, the IT windows at 6 / 2603 and 3 / 4541 retransmissions. The retransmission rule did what it says; what it measured was the reference machine, which is why the real-window run stays on a real machine rather than a runner, and why its verdict is read as a measurement. Evidence for #27 from the same runs: in both unreachable-targets cases the TCP row for `192.0.2.1:9` carries `Cause: No answer within the tool's own time limit … [ToolTimeout]` above `TCP connection timed out (more than 4000 ms).`, in the report's language — the path this release adds, executed on the real machine.

**CI.** The pull request's run on `windows-latest` (`parse,validator,guards,unit,report,envguard,launcher,campaign,gui-headless -Package -SkipGui`): green in 2 min 35 s, run 33995408199 — the `launcher` step's first run on a hosted runner.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #15, round 1 · 2026-09-06: no findings on commit `9468865`** ("Didn't find any major issues", zero inline comments). The loop closed after one round; the entry above this paragraph, a documentation-only commit, was not put through another.

## Acceptance on a second machine · 2026-09-05 — backlog #19, machine 1 of 2: Windows 11 Home (en-GB), VMware

**Machine.** `DESKTOP-5M1K8VU`, a VMware Workstation guest with one e1000e adapter (`Intel(R) 82574L Gigabit Network Connection`, `HardwareInterface=True`, classified Physical by the shipped rule — checklist M10) on NAT, no wireless adapter (`wlansvc` not running — the zero-`wifi`-row rule). Microsoft Windows 11 Home 25H2, build 26200.9168, EditionID `Core`: no `gpedit.msc`, no `secpol.msc`, no AppLocker. Formats, display language and system locale en-GB (dates `05/09/2026`), active code page 850, time zone Taipei. Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100.9168, FullLanguage. 96 DPI; 1668 × 999 under VMware Autofit, working area 1668 × 951; the Desktop redirected into OneDrive. The asset `NetworkHealthCheck-1.2.2.zip`, SHA256 `5dde92c7…` — the digest in the release notes, matched — first copied in from the host and later downloaded again with Edge inside the VM, because a copy carries no Mark of the Web and `M2` measures the warning that mark produces.

**Method.** The guided campaign `win11-enUS` of `tests/Invoke-AcceptanceCampaign.ps1`, started 16:05 on the driver of PR #11 and finished 21:46 on the driver of PR #14: the campaign was the first real use of the driver, its findings became PR #12, #13 and #14 along the way, and the state carried across the three drivers — `-Redo` (PR #13) ran `M4`, `M1`, `M2` and `M3` again once their causes were fixed, the earlier attempts kept under `superseded\`. `M8` was applied through the registry (Home has no Group Policy editor); the standard-user session ran as a local account `nhc-test`. Bundle `nhc-campaign_DESKTOP-5M1K8VU_win11-enUS_20260905_214643.zip` (with `campaign.json`, `answers.log`, the four acceptance bundles, the screenshots, the launcher outputs and the superseded attempts), kept with the release records.

**Result: 10 of 11 scenarios PASS, none FAIL; `M9` SKIPPED — AppLocker does not exist on Home.**

| Scenario | Result | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| A1 baseline (administrator, NAT) | PASS | the acceptance runner: 30 cases, 542 s — parse 0 × 2, unit 163 × 2, report 103 × 2, envguard 13 × 2, gui-headless 4, gui 4 real windows (the IT windows 145 s and 142 s; the zh-TW IT run `需要注意` on a lossy NAT — a measurement), 5 console acceptance runs, resultset 41, both root launchers |
| A2 the same as a standard user (`nhc-test`) | PASS | 30 cases, 494 s — the package needs no administrator rights, the promise the README makes |
| A3 host-only (an address, no gateway) | PASS | 10 cases, the root launchers skipped (`-SkipGui`); every report `Problem Detected`: `gateway-config` FAIL, `ping-gateway` FAIL (`AUTO_GATEWAY` resolves to nothing), DNS timed out, `[SocketError NetworkUnreachable]`, `[WebExceptionStatus Timeout]`, no gateway rows; the `routes` row `ERROR` — backlog #27 |

Keep all files in the same folder.", `LauncherError.txt` beside it in `%TEMP%\9a4d2b74-…_NetworkHealthCheck-1.2.2.zip.515\…\en-US\`; the view held two files, the launcher and its error file; no report |

You cannot run this script on the current system…", `UnauthorizedAccess`; launcher exit 1; no environment report — the case the guard cannot reach, as specified; MachinePolicy back to `Undefined`, verified |

Every report shows what the checklist demands: one `adapter` row per connected adapter with its type, one `wifi` row saying no wireless interface, gateway rows naming the gateway (none where there is none), `FullLanguage` in the PowerShell row, classified `[SocketError …]` / `[WebExceptionStatus …]` causes wherever a target failed, no stack trace in HTML or TXT (the A3 error row's diagnostics stay in the JSON), schema 2 with the `EntryPoint` of the launcher used. M6 in the direction this machine allows — en-GB Windows running the zh-TW package — the `Cause:` line in zh-TW (`原因：無法解析 URL 中的主機名稱。 [WebExceptionStatus NameResolutionFailure]`) above the operating system's English message.

**What the machine showed about the tool (1.2.2).** (1) The `IPv4 default routes` row is `ERROR — The route table could not be read.` on a machine without a default route: `Get-NetRoute … -ErrorAction Stop` throws instead of returning nothing — backlog #27, a normal state written as an engine error, IT-scoped so the verdict is unchanged. (2) The tool's own timeouts (DNS, TCP) show `Error type: System.Management.Automation.RuntimeException` in their details instead of a `Cause:` line — with #27. (3) `LauncherError.txt` always suggests extracting the ZIP, whatever stopped the launcher — backlog #28. (4) With the adapter disconnected, `routes` still lists the default route while the gateway rows say none — each row correct, together confusing; noted. Nothing else: the classification of the VMware NIC, the zero-`wifi`-row rule, the console fallback, the two guards and the launchers behaved as designed.

**What the machine showed about the campaign driver** — all fixed in the same day: the display size read from a cache (PR #12); `M1` specified for an outcome stock Windows never produces, the Windows 11 view folder's shape and hex suffix, its deletion when the launched program exits, and no way to run a recorded scenario again (PR #13, #14); the extraction to the folder the Extract All dialog proposes, a ZIP copied in without a mark, an Unblock that did not take, the missing `gpedit.msc`, a mistyped command in the other session, and Windows 11's extraction stamping the files with the archive's own times (PR #14). Every one of these is now caught before it costs a run or is stated in the record.

**Gaps of #19 closed by this machine:** a system locale opposite to the report language in one direction (en-GB Windows, zh-TW package); no wireless adapter, a virtual NIC, a Home edition without the policy consoles; the package as a standard user. **Still open:** Windows 10 (machine 2 of 2, `win10-zhTW`: Windows 10 Pro zh-TW, the other direction of M6, `gpedit` present), and AppLocker enforcement, which only Enterprise or Education can give.
## The guided acceptance campaign · 2026-09-05 — backlog #24 (`tests/`; the shipped package untouched)

**Change** (PR #11, closes backlog #24). The checklist of #19 was a list a person walks with the runner beside them: eleven scenarios, several of which change the machine and must be put back, each wanting the right command under the right label and evidence the person remembers to take. `tests/Invoke-AcceptanceCampaign.ps1` walks it instead, one campaign per machine. An automated scenario runs by itself (`A1`, the baseline through the acceptance runner; `A2`, the same as a standard user). One that changes the machine — the display at 1366 × 768 (`M4`), host-only (`A3`), the adapter disconnected (`A4`), `__PSLockdownPolicy` (`M7`), a Group Policy execution policy of AllSigned (`M8`), AppLocker (`M9`) — prints the instruction in English and zh-TW, waits for `done`, and **verifies the machine before anything runs**: the primary screen is 1366 × 768, there is an address and no gateway, there is no address at all, the machine environment carries `__PSLockdownPolicy=4`, `MachinePolicy` reads `AllSigned` in a new process, the Script rules are enforced and the Application Identity service runs — and asks again, or lets the person skip, when it is not; when the scenario is over it asks for the change to be reverted and verifies that too, and will not go on until it is. One that needs a person's click never performs the click: `M1` collects the reports a run from inside the compressed-folder view wrote, out of `%TEMP%\Temp1_*.zip\` before the view deletes them, and looks for the startup warning row; `M2` takes a screenshot the moment the person says a Windows prompt is on screen, records what it was, and reads the Mark of the Web on the download and on the extracted launcher; `M3` reads the mark after the Unblock, collects both root launchers' reports and asks whether Open Report opened the browser. Under the three policies it starts the shipped console launcher the way a double-click does (stdin from NUL, so its pause returns) and asserts what a user would see — the launcher's exit code, `LauncherError.txt` naming the script's exit code, an environment report naming the mode or its absence, no report — which is what backlog #23 waits for. Every result goes to `campaign.json` the moment it is known; the same command continues an interrupted campaign, the standard-user scenario is left `PENDING` with the exact command for that session, and the state, the asset, the extracted package and a copy of `tests\` live under `C:\Users\Public`, which every account can reach. Each invocation ends by rebuilding `nhc-campaign_<computer>_<campaign>.zip` — `campaign_summary.md`, `campaign.json`, `answers.log`, one folder per scenario with its evidence — and the exit code is the failures on record plus the scenarios this invocation selected and could not finish; a skip, an unmet precondition and `-SkipGui` are `SKIPPED` and not counted. `-Answers` replays a campaign without a person, which is how `tests/selftest_campaign.ps1` drives the state machine in the chain's new `campaign` step (CI included). With it: `gui_check.ps1 -Screenshot`, a capture of the primary screen once the window is up; `Invoke-PackageAcceptance.ps1 -ChainSteps`, so that a network scenario runs `acceptance,resultset` only; and the wrapper's bundle paths shortened.

**Measured on the reference machine.** The self-test, 26 assertions in 224 s including the baseline for real: a new campaign on `M4`, `A3`, `A4` under `-SkipGui` records `M4 SKIPPED — -SkipGui` before its gate and `A3` / `A4` `SKIPPED — precondition not met` (this machine has a gateway and addresses), writes the state, the summary (`0 passed, 0 failed, 3 skipped, 8 pending`), the answers log and the bundle, and exits 0; a resume on `A3` prints `recorded earlier`, runs nothing, keeps the finish time and logs the invocation; a quit at the `A3` gate leaves `A3` `PENDING — quit by the user` and `A4` `PENDING — not reached`, prints the resume command and exits 2; an unknown scenario id is refused by name; `M1` answered `done` without a run from the view is a `FAIL` with the reason and exit 1; and `A1` for real (`-SkipGui`) is `PASS` with the acceptance runner's own `24 passed, 0 failed, 0 not run, 2 skipped; 145 s` and its bundle in the scenario folder. `gui_check.ps1 -Screenshot` on the IT window: a 292 123-byte PNG of the 1536 × 960 screen, the window 1175 × 1090 in it, the run and the Close unchanged. Two things came out of building it. Without a byte-order mark the driver did not parse — 78 errors, because Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads a mark-less file in the system code page (950 here) and the zh-TW prompts broke the string boundaries — the reason two test files already carried one; it carries one now, and `tests/README.md` says which three files must. And the first `-Full` run failed at the acceptance bundle with `Could not find a part of the path`: the work dir of that run sat 140 characters deep, and `A1\work\bundle\chain\stage\console\en-US\Reports\<report>.json` took the deepest path past the 260 characters Windows PowerShell can address — the bundle's report paths lost `stage\` and `Reports\`, the wrapper warns up front when a work dir is longer than 120 characters, and under `%TEMP%` the deepest path of a campaign is 170. That run also took 509 s where the same steps take 120: the machine was busy, and the re-run measured 145 s. The chain with the new step, in the selection GitHub Actions runs (`parse,validator,guards,unit,report,envguard,campaign,gui-headless -Package -SkipGui`): 16 cases, 0 failed, 33 s, the `campaign` step 12.5 s for its 23 assertions.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #11, round 1 · 2026-09-05: four P2 findings, all accepted.** Every one was a promise the driver's text made and its code did not keep. The resume command printed for a partial campaign (`-Scenarios A3,A4`) dropped the subset, so following it would have run the whole plan; it carries the same subset now, checked against the plan's ids before anything else. `M4` changed the display to 1366 × 768 and had no revert, against the guarantee that every change is put back and verified; it now records the size it found before the change and asks for that size back, verified against the record. `M3` recorded `PASS` when the person answered `not clicked` to the Open Report question, certifying an item that had not been exercised; only `yes` passes, `no` and `not clicked` fail with their reason. And a scenario that changed the machine was recorded `PASS` before the revert prompt, so a campaign killed or quit at that prompt would have resumed past it and could have exited 0 with the adapter disconnected or a machine-wide policy in force; the action's outcome is now kept aside and the scenario stays `PENDING` — counted in the exit code — until the revert is verified, and a resume goes straight to the revert rather than running the action again. The self-test grew a case for the last one from a crafted state: `A4` recorded as run and unreverted, `quit` at the revert leaves it `PENDING — NOT REVERTED - quit` with exit code 1 and the action not run again; `done` with a gateway present verifies the revert, the scenario takes the action's `PASS`, `Reverted: yes - 1 gateway(s)`, exit code 0. 29 assertions, all passed.

**Round 2 · 2026-09-05, on `6be8590`: four P2 findings, all accepted — the same two promises, in the places round 1 had not reached.** The revert promise: `A3` (host-only) had no revert of its own, so a campaign that ran it alone, or skipped `A4`, could exit 0 with the machine off the internet — it ends with the adapter back on NAT, verified like `A4`'s; and `M4`'s record of the original screen size was taken again on every attempt, so a campaign interrupted at the gate after the person had already changed the display would have resumed with 1366 × 768 written down as the original and "verified" the revert against it — the facts are recorded once and a resume keeps them (self-test case 8: a crafted `1111x999` survives a resume, and the driver says so). The evidence promise: `M4` passed on the observer's default `unsure`, and `M3` passed on a self-reported `yes` with no file behind it, against the checklist's own rule that an observation without evidence does not count — `M4` passes only on `yes`, and `M3`'s question became the moment of evidence: the person brings the browser showing the HTML report to the front and answers `shown`, a screenshot is taken then, and only that passes; `failed` and `not clicked` fail with their reason. 32 assertions, all passed.

**Round 3 · 2026-09-05, on `d24103a`: three P2 findings, all accepted — the revert promise a third time, at its edges.** A skip after the person had answered `done` — the change applied, the precondition unhappy with it, the person giving up — was finalized `SKIPPED` without the revert, so a half-applied change could stay on the machine while the campaign exited 0; once `done` has been answered, a skip goes through the revert check like a finished scenario (self-test: `A3` and `A4`, skipped after an attempt on a machine with a gateway, now carry `Reverted: yes - 1 gateway(s)`), and only a skip before any attempt leaves at once (case 9). `M9`'s instruction starts the Application Identity service and sets it to start automatically, and its revert put back only the rules; it now records the service's startup type and state before the change and asks for them back, verified. And `M9`'s revert check said `Ok` when the policy could not be read at all — the precondition had proved it readable, so a policy that cannot be read now leaves the scenario pending instead of certifying the revert. Tightening the self-test for these caught one more thing of the round-1 design: a scenario finalized after its revert carried the pending-phase text ("ran (PASS); the change is still to be reverted") as its detail, because the pending record had overwritten the scenario's own — the outcome's detail now lives in its own field until the revert finalizes it. 37 assertions, all passed.

**Round 4 · 2026-09-05, on `c8f4850`: four P2 findings, all accepted — the same two promises, one level further out.** The attempt that round 3 made a skip revert on was a local variable: a quit or a crash after the first `done` forgot it, and a skip on resume was finalized without the revert again — it is recorded in the state the moment `done` is first answered, and a resume restores it (self-test case 10: a crafted quit-after-attempt, then `skip` on resume, goes through the revert check). `M9`'s revert check rejected only enforced Script rules, while its instruction asks for the rules deleted; rules left in another mode now fail it by count. And two evidence gaps against the checklist's own columns: `M1` copied the reports out of the view but took no screenshot of the window running from inside it, and `M3` had the browser but not the application window — `M1` now asks for `done` the moment the window is up and takes the screenshot then, before waiting for the run to finish; `M3` asks for `done` with the zh-TW window still open after its run, takes that screenshot, and only then asks for Open Report and the browser's. 40 assertions, all passed.

**Round 5 · 2026-09-05, on `8cab026`: one P1 and two P2 — the P1 and one P2 accepted, one P2 answered by correcting the checklist.** The P1 is the best finding of the loop: the policy scenarios cut the branch the campaign sits on. A campaign interrupted after `M7` leaves every new PowerShell in ConstrainedLanguage, where the driver's own first `New-Object` is refused; after `M8` the unsigned driver does not start at all — so the resume command the driver prints could not have performed the revert it exists for, and a machine-wide restriction could have stayed. The way back must not need PowerShell: before any policy is applied the driver now writes `RECOVER.txt` (`reg delete` for M7, gpedit for M8, secpol and the recorded service state for M9, then the resume command) and `undo-M7.cmd` into the state folder, names them in the M7 / M8 / M9 instructions before the person answers `done`, puts them in the bundle, and a driver started in ConstrainedLanguage stops at the door with exit code 3 pointing at them (self-test case 12 runs the resume in ConstrainedLanguage and gets exit 3, the mode named, the notes named and no `New-Object` error; case 11 checks the two files' contents). The P2 on `M9`: an enforced Script collection with no rule lets every script run, so the experiment would have recorded a product `FAIL` while AppLocker enforced nothing — the precondition now requires at least one rule. The other P2 asked for a screenshot as `M8`'s evidence, because the checklist's column said so; the driver keeps the launcher's console output verbatim — what the user sees, in the machine's language, searchable — and a screenshot of the same console would show less, so the checklist's M7–M9 evidence columns were corrected to name the captured output, `LauncherError.txt` and the environment report, with the reason. 44 assertions, all passed.

**Round 6 · 2026-09-05, on `06f1615`: two P1 — one accepted, one rejected with a measurement.** Accepted: `M9` told the person to create AppLocker's default Script rules, and those include a rule that allows `BUILTIN\Administrators` every script — the campaign runs as an administrator, so the launcher's script would have been allowed, the tool would have run normally, and the gold-standard experiment of #18 would have recorded a product `FAIL` while AppLocker did exactly what it was told. The instruction now says to delete that rule, and the precondition asks AppLocker itself: `Test-AppLockerPolicy` on the shipped script where the campaign keeps it, for the account running the test, must not return `Allowed` — otherwise the scenario says which rule exempts the account and does not run. Rejected: that a standard user resuming for `A2` could read but not write the state an administrator created under `C:\Users\Public`. Measured with `icacls` on the reference machine: `C:\Users\Public` carries `NT AUTHORITY\INTERACTIVE:(OI)(CI)(IO)(M,DC)`, and a folder and a file created there by the administrator inherit `INTERACTIVE:(I)(OI)(CI)(M,DC)` and `INTERACTIVE:(I)(M,DC)` — every interactively logged-on account, a standard user included, has Modify on them. That is the reason `C:\Users\Public` was chosen for the state; the code is unchanged, and the rejection stands on the pull request for a person to confirm.

**Round 7 · 2026-09-05, on `d60a023`: two P2, both accepted.** The round-6 check asked AppLocker about the script in the extracted package, while the launcher runs a copy under `M9\en-US` — under a path rule the two can differ; `M9`'s preparation now makes that copy first and the check asks about the copy, the path that is executed. And a gate answer outside `done` / `skip` / `quit` — `Done`, a typo — had been treated as a skip, which the exit code does not count, so a scenario the person meant to run could vanish silently: answers are matched case-insensitively, the console asks again, and an unrecognized value in an answers file leaves the scenario `PENDING` with the value named (self-test case 13: `A4=DONE` is `done`, `A4=dnoe` is `PENDING — unrecognized answer 'dnoe'` with exit code 1). A preparation that throws now fails the scenario instead of the driver. 48 assertions, all passed.

**Round 8 · 2026-09-05, on `d011757`: one P2, accepted.** The round-7 branch for an unrecognized revert answer left the scenario `PENDING` and went on to the next one — with the display, the network or a machine-wide policy possibly still changed, against the guarantee that a revert is verified before the next scenario starts. It stops the campaign now, like a quit, and so does a revert the answers file cannot verify (self-test case 14: an invalid revert answer for a scenario awaiting its revert leaves it `PENDING`, the next selected scenario `PENDING — not reached`, exit code 2). 51 assertions, all passed.

**Round 9 · 2026-09-05, on `4ecbdb0`: two P2, both accepted — the recorded attempt against the branches that leave early.** `-SkipGui` dropped a real-window scenario before looking at whether it had been attempted, so a campaign interrupted after the display was changed and resumed with `-SkipGui` finalized `M4` as `SKIPPED` with the display still changed; the drop now goes through the revert first (self-test case 15: an attempted `M4` whose recorded size equals the screen, resumed with `-SkipGui`, is reverted-verified and then `SKIPPED — -SkipGui after an attempt`, exit 0). And an unrecognized gate answer after an attempt went on to the next scenario, saying nothing had run; after an attempt it stops the campaign like an unconfirmed revert, and only before any attempt does it move on (case 16: an attempted `A4` with `A4=dnoe` leaves `A4` pending, `M7` not reached, exit code 2). Every branch of the scenario runner that leaves before the attempt is examined was walked for this round: the two session-mismatch branches leave a scenario pending for the other session, which is correct — a standard user cannot revert a policy — and these two were the rest. 56 assertions, all passed.

**Round 10 · 2026-09-05, on `5f4e437`: three P2, all accepted.** A scenario left pending with its change possibly on the machine could be stepped around: a later invocation selecting only another scenario never reached its revert and ran the new scenario on the changed machine — every invocation now handles such scenarios first, whatever was selected, and in a standard-user session, where those reverts cannot be made, runs nothing until the administrator has resumed (self-test case 17: an `A4` awaiting its revert with only `M7` selected is reverted first and finalized `PASS` with its own detail before `M7` runs). `campaign.json` was overwritten in place, so a crash mid-save could leave half a file where the next resume — the one that has to revert a machine-wide policy — would fail to read it; it is written to a temporary file and swapped in atomically, the previous version kept as `campaign.json.bak`, and an unreadable state names the backup (case 18). And `M3`, the scenario after the Unblock, recorded the ZIP's mark in its detail without requiring it gone — a ZIP that still carries the mark now fails the scenario. 61 assertions, all passed.

**Round 11 · 2026-09-05, on `c39722d`: two P2, both accepted.** `M1` passed on any `startup` row, so a regression of the archive detection would have gone unnoticed as long as some other startup notice appeared; it passes only on the compressed-folder warning itself, matched in either language. And `M2`, the extraction without Unblock, recorded a download without the Mark of the Web and could still pass, measuring nothing of the warning it exists for; a prerequisite is now checked before the person is asked to do anything — the download must carry the mark — and the scenario is skipped with the reason otherwise (self-test case 19: the self-test's built asset carries no mark, so `M2` is `SKIPPED — prerequisite not met` with no gate asked). 63 assertions, all passed.

**Round 12 · 2026-09-05, on `c4d2117`: two P2, both accepted.** The round-11 prerequisite was skipped for a scenario already attempted, so an `M2` interrupted after its `done` and resumed after the ZIP had been unblocked (by `M3` in another subset, say) bypassed the check — the prerequisite is evaluated on every attempt, a resume included, and `M2`'s action itself now fails when the download carries no mark at the time of the run (self-test case 20: an attempted `M2` on an unmarked download is skipped on resume; case 21 gives a copy of the built asset a `Zone.Identifier` stream and runs `M2` end to end — the prompts run, the screenshot is taken, and the scenario fails for the missing extraction and report with `download mark: ZoneId=3` in its detail). And the `M9` revert instruction said `sc config AppIDSvc start= <manual|…>`, which `sc.exe` refuses — the value for a manual start is `demand`, as `RECOVER.txt`'s mapping already had it; the instruction says `<auto|demand|disabled>` and points at the notes for the recorded values. Case 21 earned its place at once: on its first run `M2`'s action threw `cannot bind argument to parameter 'LiteralPath' because it is null` — the actions of `M2` and `M3` read the extraction folders from variables local to the function that builds the plan, and a PowerShell scriptblock captures nothing, so at run time, long after that function has returned, both were `$null`; on a real campaign both scenarios would have failed with that exception instead of measuring anything. The folders are script-scope variables now, and case 21 asserts the scenario's real detail. 68 assertions, all passed.

**Round 13 · 2026-09-05, on `0cdbad9`: two P2, both accepted.** `M2` accepted any `Zone.Identifier` stream as the mark — a malformed one, or a local or intranet zone — none of which carries the warning it measures; the prerequisite and the action now require the Internet or Restricted zone (`ZoneId=3` or `4`; self-test case 23: a copy of the asset marked `ZoneId=0` is refused). And `-SkipGui` dropped only the real-window scenario, while `M1`, `M2` and `M3` need a desktop just as much — a person's clicks and the screenshots — so a campaign in a session without one would have stopped at a prompt or failed on the capture; the three are dropped with `M4` now (case 22), and the self-test's own desktop cases run without `-SkipGui`, as they should have. 72 assertions, all passed.

**Round 14 · 2026-09-05, on `f8d76be`: two P2, both accepted.** In a standard-user session with `-SkipGui` the desktop scenarios met the "left for the administrator session" check before the drop and were left pending — counted — instead of `SKIPPED — -SkipGui`; the drop now comes before the session is looked at, for a scenario not attempted (an attempted one with a revert still waits for the administrator). And a bundle that could not be written raised the exit code in memory after `campaign_summary.md` had been written, so the record on disk contradicted the invocation; the summary is a function now, called again with a `bundle … FAIL` row when the bundle fails (self-test case 24 holds the bundle's path open from another process: the row appears on disk, the summary line counts it, exit code 1). 75 assertions, all passed.

**Round 15 · 2026-09-05, on `72cef1e`: two P2, both accepted.** `M8` passed on any nonzero launcher exit without a report — a crash, or a ConstrainedLanguage imposed by some other policy, would have passed as the AllSigned refusal; it now requires the signature refusal itself in the captured output (`UnauthorizedAccess` / `SecurityError`, or the message in either language) and no environment report, since one would mean the script started. And a bundle rebuild that failed — the locked path of case 24, precisely — left the previous `nhc-campaign_*.zip` at the advertised path looking current; the bundle now carries the invocation's time in its name, the summary names it (`- Bundle: …`), earlier bundles are removed on success, and when the bundle cannot be written the summary says `NOT WRITTEN` and that an older file does not carry this invocation (case 24 now locks a staged file instead, and asserts the header line and that no bundle appeared). 77 assertions, all passed.

**Round 16 · 2026-09-05, on `fb5b593`: two P2, both accepted and deferred — the loop closed here by the owner's decision.** The two are refinements of round 15's own fixes: `M8` accepted PowerShell's generic `SecurityError` / `UnauthorizedAccess` classification as the signature refusal, which an AppLocker or Software Restriction Policy block emits as well, so the check should require the signature-specific message (in each display language the campaign meets) and re-read `MachinePolicy` after the run; and the bundle's name carries the time to the second, so two invocations in one second would collide and the second would record a bundle failure — the name should carry milliseconds and the process id. Both are backlog #25. **The loop in numbers: 16 rounds, 36 findings — 34 accepted and fixed, each with a self-test case that reproduces it (the `campaign` step grew from 23 to 77 assertions), 1 rejected with a measurement (the `icacls` output for `C:\Users\Public`, for a person to confirm), 1 answered by correcting the checklist it cited (the M8 screenshot), and the two deferred.** Two threads ran through the sixteen rounds: every change to the machine is reverted and verified before anything else runs — the pending-until-reverted record, the recorded attempt, the outstanding revert that comes first whatever was selected, the stop on anything short of a verified revert, `-SkipGui` after the revert, the way back without PowerShell; and a manual observation certifies an item only with an explicit yes and a file — the screenshots of `M1`, `M3` and `M4`, the Internet-zone mark, the compressed-folder warning itself, the signature refusal itself. Four defects the review did not find were found by the self-tests written for its findings: the driver's byte-order mark, the 260-character path, the pending text overwriting a scenario's detail, and the `M2` / `M3` actions reading a function's locals at run time — the last of which would have crashed both scenarios on the first real campaign.

**Field fix · 2026-09-05, PR #12, found by the first campaign on the Windows 11 VM (`win11-enUS`).** `A1` passed (30 cases, 542 s); at `M4`'s gate the person set the display to 1366 × 768 and the precondition answered three times with the size from before, `1668x999`. The driver measured through `Screen.PrimaryScreen.Bounds`, and Windows Forms builds its `Screen` objects on the first call and keeps them until a `WM_DISPLAYCHANGE` reaches its `SystemEvents` window — on the STA thread `powershell.exe` runs the pipeline on, that window is served only while the thread pumps messages, which it never does while the script waits in `Read-Host` (verified on the reference machine: apartment STA, the `Screen.screens` cache populated after one read, `SystemEvents.windowThread` null). So the first measurement of a campaign was the only one — and the revert check would have accepted anything, the cached value always equalling the recorded one. `Get-PrimaryBounds` now reads `SystemInformation.PrimaryMonitorSize`, a `GetSystemMetrics` call on every access, and `Save-Screenshot` measures through it; self-test case 25 guards the driver on its AST (no `[Screen]` type expression anywhere in it; `[SystemInformation]::PrimaryMonitorSize` read as a statement inside `Get-PrimaryBounds`; `Save-Screenshot` calling it; 77 → 81 assertions). The campaign on the VM continues with the fixed driver: the outstanding `M4` revert first, then `M4` again. Unchanged, and a known limit: the size is the one the process sees — `powershell.exe` is not DPI-aware, so at a display scale other than 100 % the values are scaled (the reference machine at 125 % reports 1536 × 960 for its 1920 × 1200).

**Round 1 (PR #12) · 2026-09-05, on `6fb0abd`: one P3 — the recommendation taken, the premise corrected.** Codex read case 25's regular expression as already matching the explanatory comment inside `Get-PrimaryBounds`; it did not — the comment says `SystemInformation.PrimaryMonitorSize` with a dot, the pattern required `]::` — but a text match is the wrong instrument for a guard on code all the same: a later comment could satisfy the positive assertion or trip the negative one. The case now inspects the AST, as the guards of backlog #17 do: no `TypeExpressionAst` naming `Windows.Forms.Screen` anywhere in the driver; a static `MemberExpressionAst` on `Windows.Forms.SystemInformation` with the member `PrimaryMonitorSize` inside the body of `Get-PrimaryBounds`; a `CommandAst` calling `Get-PrimaryBounds` inside the body of `Save-Screenshot`. The next round is the owner's call; CI on `6fb0abd`: 16 steps passed, the `campaign` step 80 assertions.

**Field findings · 2026-09-05, PR #13, from the same first campaign (`win11-enUS`).** *`M1` was specified for an outcome stock Windows never produces.* From the compressed-folder view Explorer extracts only the file double-clicked into a temporary folder — on this Windows 11 `%TEMP%\388e11bd-…-27df0c2ad684_NetworkHealthCheck-1.2.2.zip.684\NetworkHealthCheck-1.2.2\en-US\`, holding `Start-NetworkCheck.cmd` alone — so the launcher's first check (`if not exist "%~dp0NetworkHealthCheck.ps1"`) stops it with "The program file NetworkHealthCheck.ps1 is missing. Keep all files in the same folder.", `LauncherError.txt` beside it and a pause; the tool never starts and no report exists. The checklist expected a report carrying the compressed-folder warning row and the driver accepted nothing else — but that row is the script's second line of defence, reached only where the whole folder is extracted (another archiver, or a `.ps1` double-clicked earlier in the same view). By the owner's decision the specification now names both outcomes as the package behaving as designed — the launcher's own stop on stock Windows, the warning row where the tool runs — and the driver passes on either, copying `LauncherError.txt` and a listing of the view folder out before the view deletes them, and fails on a launcher stopped for any other reason or on nothing in any view folder (self-test case 26: three crafted views under a redirected `%TEMP%`). *The Windows 11 view folder has a new shape*, `<guid>_<name>.zip.<n>`, which the driver's search (`Temp1_*.zip`) did not match; it matches both shapes now. The shipped script's own `Test-IsRunningFromArchive` (`\.zip[\\/]`) does not match it either — a release change, recorded as backlog #26. *A recorded scenario could not be run again*: `M4`, skipped under the cached size of PR #12, stayed `SKIPPED` on every resume. `-Redo <ids>` clears the record, moves the earlier evidence to `superseded\<id>_<time>` (bundled) and names what was superseded in the summary; a scenario pending with a change possibly still on the machine is refused until its revert is verified (self-test case 27). 81 → 96 assertions.

**Round 1 (PR #13) · 2026-09-05, on `ee0a62b`: one P2, accepted.** `-Redo A1,A4` with `A1` complete and `A4` pending a revert moved `A1`'s evidence to `superseded\` and then refused `A4` — before the state was saved, so `campaign.json` still described evidence that was no longer where it said. Every id is checked before anything moves, and each record is saved the moment its evidence has moved, so a refusal or a crash between two ids leaves the state and the folders agreeing (self-test case 28: the refused `-Redo A3,A4` leaves `A3`'s folder, record and history untouched; 96 → 99 assertions).

**Field findings 2 · 2026-09-05, PR #14, from the rest of the first campaign (`win11-enUS`, Windows 11 Home).** *The extraction went to the wrong folder and the scenario measured nothing.* `M3` asked for an extraction into `Desktop\NHC-M3`; Explorer's Extract All dialog proposes `<folder of the ZIP>\<name of the ZIP>\`, the person accepted it, both launchers ran from there, and the scenario — which looked under `NHC-M3` only — failed with "no report" although the Unblock and Open Report had been done. `M2` and `M3` now go in two steps: the Unblock (or not) and the extraction first, verified before anyone double-clicks (the launcher below the named folder, the mark gone for `M3`), the launcher step after; a report still missing is reported with any report the run left elsewhere below the work root, the Desktop and Downloads, so that the failure names the place. `-WorkRoot` moves the folders off the Desktop — the VM's is OneDrive-redirected — and lets the self-test leave the real desktop alone (cases 21, 29, 30). *Windows Home.* `gpedit.msc` does not exist there, so `M8`'s instruction could not be followed; the same MachinePolicy was applied with two `reg add` lines under `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Policies\Microsoft\Windows\PowerShell` (what the policy writes, what PowerShell reads) and the scenario passed on the signature refusal. The driver now records the edition (`Get-CimInstance Win32_OperatingSystem`, `EditionID`, whether `gpedit.msc` and `secpol.msc` exist) in the state and the summary, shows both ways for `M8` and names the one this machine takes — kept in the scenario's facts and in `RECOVER.txt` — and skips `M9` before anyone is asked where AppLocker does not exist (Home), noting on Pro that rules are held but not enforced (case 31, on a crafted Home state). *The command for the standard-user session was mistyped* (a long line typed by hand across sessions): it is written to `run-as-standard-user.cmd` next to the state as well (case 32). *A ZIP copied into the VM has no Mark of the Web*, so `M2`'s prerequisite skipped it, correctly — the hint now says so: download the asset with the browser of the machine under test. 99 → 113 assertions.

**Round 1 (PR #14) · 2026-09-05, on `9565c6a`: one P1 and two P2, all accepted.** *P1 - a stale extraction tree passed the new precondition.* The check looked for the launcher by name only, so a `NHC-M3` left by an earlier campaign - another release, or for `M2` another download's marks - would have been accepted and its launchers run, and the fresh reports would have certified the wrong package. `Test-ExtractedPackage` now requires the launchers to have been created after the scenario started (a tree from before is refused with "remove the folder and extract again") and the extracted `en-US\NetworkHealthCheck.ps1` to be byte-identical to the campaign's asset (SHA256 against the state's package copy; a differing file names the version it carries). Self-test case 33; cases 21 and 30 stamp their prepared trees as created later, the way they stamp reports. *P2 - `-WorkRoot` was lost on resume.* Neither the state nor the resume command kept it, so a resume without it went back to the Desktop and looked in other folders. The root is recorded in the state on first use and reused by every resume; given again with another value it wins and the change is an event; the summary names it. *P2 - the registry path of `M8` cleared what was there.* Where `ExecutionPolicy` or `EnableScripts` existed before `M8` (a policy set by other means), the instruction overwrote them and the revert deleted them, and the verification certified `Undefined` instead of the original. `M8`'s preparation now records the effective MachinePolicy and the two values as they are (data, or absent); the revert instruction is built at revert time from that record (`reg add` with the original data, or `reg delete` where a value was absent; with gpedit, back to the setting that gave the original policy), `RECOVER.txt` carries the same lines, and the verification compares with the recorded policy - a cleanup instruction may now be a scriptblock, evaluated with the scenario's facts. Self-test case 34: a crafted record that had `RemoteSigned` gets the two `reg add` lines back and is not verified while the machine says `Undefined`. A defect of my own on the way: the two new helpers were first defined after `Write-RecoveryNotes`, which runs right after its own definition, so the driver stopped at startup with `Get-M8WayBack` unknown - the first self-test case caught it. 113 → 120 assertions.

**Field finding 3 · 2026-09-05, PR #14, from the redo of `M1` on the Windows 11 VM.** With the ZIP freshly downloaded (so marked), the double-click inside the view first raised Windows' "Open File - Security Warning" for the extracted `.cmd`, then the launcher stopped for the missing program file as expected - and `M1` still failed with "nothing from a compressed-folder view". Two causes, both in the driver. *The view folder's suffix is hex, not a number*: the first folder had been `…_NetworkHealthCheck-1.2.2.zip.684`, this one was `5d2b5f20-7a4f-4428-a382-e2e558bb2bc4_NetworkHealthCheck-1.2.2.zip.bc4`, and the pattern accepted digits alone; it accepts `.zip.<word>` now (`dir %TEMP%` on the VM listed both folders). *The folder may not outlive the program started from it*: the folder of the security-warning dialog (`9ce5cbee-…`) was gone when the driver looked - Windows 11 removed it when the launcher closed - while the 16:40 folder of the first attempt was still there; the timing is not predictable. `M1` now collects its evidence the moment the person answers `done` - the launcher writes `LauncherError.txt` before it shows its message and pauses, so the file is there - and once more when a tool run has finished with its window still open, and only then says that the windows may be closed; a failure lists the folders written under `%TEMP%` since the scenario started, so that a view folder of yet another shape shows in the record. Self-test case 26 crafts the hex-suffix shape.

**Field finding 4 · 2026-09-05, PR #14, from the redo of `M3` on the Windows 11 VM.** `M1` passed with the fix above (the launcher's stop and `LauncherError.txt` collected while the message was on screen; the view folder this time `…zip.515`). `M3` then refused a freshly extracted `NHC-M3` as "extracted before this scenario started (created 2026-09-04 16:44:00)": Windows 11's Extract All gives the extracted files the archive's own timestamps - creation time included, hence a date from the day the asset was built - so the round-1 freshness test on the launcher's creation time can never pass there. Freshness is now read off the folder the person was asked to extract into: it is not an archive entry, so it is written when the extraction puts the package into it, and a folder that already held the tree and was extracted into again is refused as before ("remove the folder and extract again"). Self-test cases 21, 30 and 33 stamp the folder instead of the launchers.

**Round 2 (PR #14) · 2026-09-05, on `ad41c10`: two P2, both accepted.** *The edition self-test assumed the host.* Case 31 asserted that the first `M9` run reaches the gate - true on Pro, Enterprise and the hosted runner, false on a Home host, where the new prerequisite skips `M9` first and the self-test would have failed on the very edition the change is about. The case now crafts the state for both directions - a Pro record (the gate reached, the note that Pro holds rules without enforcing them) and a Home record (skipped before the gate, the count of gate answers unchanged) - and asserts nothing about the host's own edition beyond the summary line. *The registry restore lost the values' kinds.* `M8`'s snapshot kept the data as strings and the way back always wrote `REG_SZ` / `REG_DWORD`, so a value stored as another kind (`REG_EXPAND_SZ`, a string `1`) would have come back changed. The snapshot records each value's kind (`RegistryKey.GetValueKind`) beside its data, `Get-M8WayBack` writes `reg add /t` with that kind (String, ExpandString, DWord, QWord; any other kind is named for a restore by hand), and a record from before the kinds were recorded falls back to the kinds the policy editor writes. Case 34 crafts `RemoteSigned` as `ExpandString` and `EnableScripts` as `String` and expects `REG_EXPAND_SZ` and `REG_SZ` back. 120 assertions.

**Round 3 (PR #14) · 2026-09-05, on `236524f`: one P1 and one P2, both accepted.** *P1 - two package trees under the folder.* The precondition validated the first launcher it found and the actions read reports from anywhere below the folder, so a second, older tree beside a fresh one could still be the one run and certified. `Test-ExtractedPackage` now refuses more than one copy of a launcher below the folder ("remove the folder and extract the downloaded ZIP once") and launchers that are not in one folder, and the `M2` / `M3` actions read their reports from the validated tree - the directory of the single launcher - not from the folder at large (the failure names that tree). Self-test case 33 adds a second tree and is refused. *P2 - an existing empty value taken for an absent one.* The way back decided existence from the data, so an empty `REG_SZ` would have been deleted rather than re-created empty, while the verification - `Undefined` either way - could not tell. Existence is the recorded kind now (the data alone only for records from before the kinds were recorded), the data is quoted in the `reg add` lines, and an empty value comes back empty. Case 34 crafts an empty `ExecutionPolicy` beside an absent `EnableScripts` and expects `/d ""` for the one and `reg delete` for the other. 120 → 122 assertions.

**Round 4 (PR #14) · 2026-09-05, on `a0a796b`: two P2, both accepted - the same corner of `M8`'s snapshot.** *A value whose data reads `absent`* was still turned into an empty string on the way back, because the sentinel conversion ran whenever the value existed; with a kind recorded the data is now taken exactly as recorded, and the sentinel is read only from records made before the kinds were kept. *Non-scalar data* (`REG_MULTI_SZ`, `REG_BINARY`) was coerced to a string by the snapshot, so the regedit fallback of round 3 named data that could not restore anything. The snapshot now keeps the data in the text `reg add` takes for the kind - a multi-string joined with `\0`, binary as hex - and the way back writes those kinds too (`/t REG_MULTI_SZ`, `/t REG_BINARY`); only a kind `reg add` cannot write is left to regedit, with the recorded data named. Case 34 crafts a `REG_SZ` whose data is the word `absent` and a `REG_MULTI_SZ`, and expects both back as they were. 122 → 123 assertions.

**Not in this change.** The campaign on the machines of #19 — this is the tool for it. Not driven: A5 (run the campaign again under another name after changing the NIC type), A6 (`-SkipGui`), M5 and M6 (the runner's console cases cover them in `A1`), M10 (the `adapter` rows and the probe in `A1`'s bundle), M11 (hardware). `M2` is semi-automated on purpose — the person double-clicks and says when the prompt is up; extracting the way Explorer does (so that the mark propagates) and finding the prompt's window through UI Automation is a second phase, if the first campaign shows it is worth it. Nothing under `healthcheck/` changes but this record.

## Package acceptance with Windows PowerShell alone · 2026-09-05 — backlog #19, the runner (`tests/`; the shipped package untouched)

**Change** (PR #10, prepares backlog #19). The item asks for acceptance of the *package* along the path a user takes, on machines other than the author's, because "the whole validation chain cannot run there — it needs Python and git, which a clean machine does not have". Counted, that is three steps: `validator`, `guards` and `package`, and all three run on every commit on GitHub Actions since backlog #20. The other eight are Windows PowerShell 5.1, which a clean Windows 10 / 11 machine has. So the acceptance is one command with nothing installed: `tests/Invoke-PackageAcceptance.ps1` takes the downloaded release asset, checks it against the digest from the release notes, extracts it, checks the package against its own `SHA256SUMS.txt` (every entry recomputed with `Get-FileHash` — the validator's manifest check without Python) and the CRLF rule, writes the machine's description, runs the chain with a new `-PackageDir` parameter pointed at the extracted package and `-Steps parse,unit,report,envguard,gui-headless,gui,acceptance,resultset`, opens the package root's `Start-English.cmd` and `Start-Traditional-Chinese.cmd` through the real window (the launchers `README_BILINGUAL.md` sends people to), lists every case the chain did not reach as `NOT RUN` so that a gap is visible rather than silent (a step dropped on purpose by `-SkipGui` is `SKIPPED`, listed and not counted), and leaves one bundle next to the ZIP — summary, the chain's summary and logs, every report the runs wrote, the probe, the checklist — named by machine, label and time. Its exit code is the number of failed and not-run cases; on a machine restricted to a limited language mode it writes the probe, says so and exits 3, like the tool's own guard. `tests/environment_probe.ps1` is the description: operating system, edition and build; formats, display language, system locale, code page, time zone; PowerShell version, language mode, execution policy by scope, .NET; WDAC, AppLocker, Defender, ASR, SmartScreen and attachment-manager state; the account's rights and session; display and working area; every adapter with the flags and the description the tool classifies it by, both of the tool's IP data sources, the wireless service and `netsh wlan show interfaces` as printed, the counter classes, the proxy; and, given the package, the Mark of the Web on its files and the classification the shipped `Test-IsVirtualAdapter` gives each adapter. Each fact is read on its own try, with cmdlets, operators and property reads only — the discipline of the tool's environment report — so a locked-down machine can be described too. `tests/package-acceptance-checklist.md` holds what only a person at the desktop can see, with an expected outcome and an evidence column per item: the Windows prompts on a marked download, the run from inside the compressed-folder view, the IT window at 1366 × 768, a standard user, `__PSLockdownPolicy` (the launcher path of backlog #18 that the `envguard` simulation cannot reach), a Group Policy execution policy and AppLocker on Enterprise media. The runner gained three things with it: `-PackageDir`; a header that says "no git" / "no python" instead of failing (only the three steps above need them); and a zh-TW copy of the unreachable-targets acceptance case, so that the chain has **29 cases at release instead of 28** and, wherever it runs, one of the two reports carries the operating system's message in the other language — backlog #14 in both directions.

**Measured on the reference machine against the published 1.2.2 asset (`NetworkHealthCheck-1.2.2.zip`, 223 590 bytes, SHA256 `5dde92c7…` — the digest in the release notes, matched): 30 cases, 29 passed, 1 failed, 0 not run, 479 s.** Extraction 25 files; tool version 1.2.2 in both languages with every launcher present; `SHA256SUMS.txt` 22 entries, 0 wrong; 12 CRLF files, 0 with a stray LF; the probe written in 12.9 s. The chain against the extracted package: parser 0 × 2; unit 163 × 2; report-stage 103 × 2; language-mode guard 13 × 2; headless GUI 4 / 4 (940 × 700, 940 × 872); real windows: en-US user, zh-TW user and en-US IT passed (32 results, 6 IT, Overall Healthy; the IT window kept PingCount 30 / SampleSeconds 125 and took 143 s; the windows measure 1175 × 875 and 1175 × 1090 at this machine's 120 DPI), and **zh-TW IT failed under `-RequireHealthy` because the tool reported 檢測到異常 (FAIL), fingerprint `quality`: TCPv4 76 segments retransmitted of 3 757 sent in the 125 s sample, 2.023 %** — every other assertion on the case held (run options, the 32-row result set, the IT title, closed through its own button). That is the tool doing its job on a Wi-Fi laptop, as on 2026-09-04 (4 of 62 in an 8 s sample); it is recorded as a measurement and the step was re-run once, below. Console acceptance 5 / 5 with 32 / 34 / 32 / 35 / 35 results, the two unreachable-target cases carrying `[SocketError HostNotFound]` and `[WebExceptionStatus NameResolutionFailure]` causes; result-set self-check 41 / 41; both root launchers opened the user window of their language, started by themselves, reported Overall Healthy with 32 results and closed with exit code 0 (15.9 s, 15.4 s). The bundle: 243 167 bytes.

**The `gui` step, re-run once against the same extracted package** (`Invoke-ValidationChain.ps1 -Steps gui -RequireHealthy -PackageDir …`, the way the record handled the 2026-09-04 verdict): 4 / 4, Overall Healthy in all four windows, fingerprint `healthy`, 333 s — the zh-TW IT window included, its 125 s sample clean this time. Both runs stand: the verdict follows the network, and a `-RequireHealthy` failure on a quality verdict is the runner reading the tool correctly on a machine the record expects to be healthy.

**With `-SkipGui`** (a session without a desktop), the same asset: 24 passed, 0 failed, 0 not run, 2 skipped, 120 s — the four real-window cases and the two root launchers listed as `SKIPPED`, exit code 0. The first `-SkipGui` run, minutes earlier, had listed the root launchers as `NOT RUN` and exited 1 for a gap that was a choice; the two words now make that distinction, and only `NOT RUN` counts. Its bundle holds 49 entries: the summary, the chain's summary and logs, the two environment reports the `envguard` step wrote, the HTML / TXT / JSON reports of the five console runs, the probe and the checklist — `chain.log` with its zh-TW text intact, because the wrapper reads the chain's UTF-8 output as UTF-8.

**What the probe says about the reference machine, which corrects the description in backlog #19.** Windows 11 專業版 25H2, 26200.9168, installed in English (`OSLanguage` 1033, MUI en-US and zh-TW) and running with **zh-TW as formats, display language and system locale** (code page 950) — not "`Get-UICulture` en-US" as #19 recorded; Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100.9168, FullLanguage, execution policy `LocalMachine=RemoteSigned` with `MachinePolicy` and `UserPolicy` undefined; WDAC code-integrity policy status 2 with the user-mode policy 0, no AppLocker rule collections and the Application Identity service stopped; Defender on, 0 of 1 ASR rule in block mode; the account in the Administrators group but the session not elevated; working area 1536 × 912 at 120 DPI; seven adapters, of which the shipped rule classifies the Wi-Fi 6E AX211 and the I219-V as Physical and the two VMware host adapters, the VirtualBox host-only adapter, the Hyper-V switch and the TAP adapter as Virtual — all five by their `Virtual=True` flag, before the description is looked at; one wireless interface connected, and `netsh wlan show interfaces` captured in its zh-TW layout for the record. One line differed between two probes minutes apart on the same machine: `Win32_OperatingSystem.Caption` came back as `Microsoft Windows 11 專業版` once and `Microsoft Windows 11 Pro` the next time, with every culture line identical — WMI's localization of that string, not a change on the machine — which is why the probe also reads the edition from the registry (`Professional`), the fact that does not move. The cross-language measurement of the two unreachable cases came out one-sided here: in the zh-TW report the `Cause:` line is Chinese and the operating system's message beneath it — `The remote name could not be resolved: 'nhc-no-such-host.invalid'` — is English, so backlog #14's second direction is shown; in the en-US report both are English. This machine's .NET messages are English although its display language is Chinese, which is why #14's original observation (a Chinese `作業逾時` inside an en-US report) cannot be reproduced on it on demand, and why the zh-TW Windows VM of #19 is still needed for the first direction. **The probe in ConstrainedLanguage**, run the way the `envguard` step runs the tool: 7 of its facts come back as "unavailable", every one a cmdlet whose module the mode refuses to load (`NetAdapter`, `NetTCPIP`, `ConfigDefender` — the refusal #18 measured for `Get-NetIPConfiguration`) or one of the two facts that say they need .NET; everything else is read, the file is written, exit code 0.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #10, round 1 · 2026-09-05: one P2 finding, accepted.** When the bundle could not be written — an unwritable `-BundleDir`, a full disk — the wrapper printed the exception and exited with the count of failed and not-run cases, which can be zero: a caller would have read success from a run that had not produced the evidence it exists to produce. The bundle failure is now a `FAIL` case of its own (`bundle / nhc-acceptance_….zip`, with the work dir named in the detail, since everything is still there), the summary on disk is rewritten to carry it, and the exit code counts it. Measured with `-BundleDir` on a drive that does not exist (`-PackageDir` form, `-SkipGui`): 22 passed, then `bundle … FAIL — not written: 找不到磁碟機。名為 'Q' 的磁碟機不存在。`, summary `22 passed, 1 failed, 0 not run, 2 skipped`, exit code 1, and the summary on disk ends with that row; the same command with a writable bundle folder: 24 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped, 127 s, exit code 0. The finding is of the same family as the artifact-name one on PR #9 — a path the green runs never took, because every bundle had been written.

**Round 2 · 2026-09-05, on `2488056`: two P2 findings, both accepted.** First, the manifest check skipped every line that did not parse, so a truncated or malformed entry in `SHA256SUMS.txt` left its file unverified while the case passed on the others; every non-blank line must now be an entry. Measured with a `deadbeef  en-US/NetworkHealthCheck.ps1` line appended to a copy of the package: `package / SHA256SUMS.txt FAIL — 22 entries, 1 wrong: unparsable line: deadbeef  en-US/NetworkHealthCheck.ps1`, summary `21 passed, 1 failed, 0 not run, 2 skipped`, exit code 1. Second, a reused `-WorkDir` kept the previous invocation's `chain\summary.md`, and a chain that died before writing its own — a package it cannot read, a killed process — would have been reported with the old table; the old file is now removed before the launch and a summary older than the launch is not accepted. Measured by reusing the work dir of a passed run with a package whose en-US script had been deleted: the layout and manifest cases failed, the chain exited 1 without a summary, all 18 of its cases came out `NOT RUN — no summary.md from this invocation`, summary `2 passed, 2 failed, 18 not run, 2 skipped`, exit code 20, the stale file gone. Both belong to the family of round 1 and of PR #9's finding: inputs the green runs never had — a malformed manifest, a reused work dir — because every run so far had a clean manifest and a fresh work dir.

**Round 3 · 2026-09-05, on `a15cbfd`: two P2 findings — one rejected with evidence, one accepted.** Rejected: the probe's administrator check, said to cast the `whoami /groups` lines to `System.Object[]` so that the SID `S-1-5-32-544` is never seen and every administrator is reported as a standard user. A `[string]` cast of an array in PowerShell joins the elements with `$OFS` (a space by default) — it is `.ToString()` that yields the type name, not the cast: `[string]@('first line', 'S-1-5-32-544 here', 'third')` is `first line S-1-5-32-544 here third` and matches; the real `whoami /groups /fo csv /nh` output of this account, 13 lines, joins to 933 characters and matches; and the probe files of every run above read `Administrators group in the token … present` for this administrator account. The code is unchanged, and the rejection stands on the pull request for a person to confirm. Accepted: round 2 had removed only the stale `summary.md` from a reused `-WorkDir`; the previous invocation's `chain\*.log` and the reports under `chain\stage\` were still copied into the bundle, so a bundle could list `NOT RUN` while carrying the evidence of the run before. The chain's folder is now removed before the launch, and the top-level logs and the probe are bundled only when written since the run started. Measured by reusing the work dir of a complete run (75 files under `chain\`) with the unreadable package: the chain exited 1, `chain\` held 0 files afterwards, and the bundle contained 5 — the summary, `chain.log`, the probe and its log, the checklist — none of them from the earlier run; summary `2 passed, 2 failed, 18 not run, 2 skipped`, exit code 20. The positive control (`-SkipGui`, a fresh work dir, the published asset): 24 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped, 154 s, exit code 0, 44 files in the bundle.

**Round 4 · 2026-09-05, on `6e75a04`: one P2 finding, accepted.** The round-3 cleanup ran only immediately before the chain was launched, so a run in a reused `-WorkDir` that completed early — a digest mismatch, a failed extraction — still bundled the previous invocation's `chain\` output under a summary that said `NOT RUN`. The chain's folder is now removed first, before anything can complete early. Measured by reusing the work dir of a complete run (75 files under `chain\`) with a wrong expected digest: `asset … FAIL — MISMATCH`, `extract and everything after it — NOT RUN`, summary `0 passed, 1 failed, 1 not run, 0 skipped`, exit code 2, `chain\` gone, and a bundle of 2 files — the summary and the checklist. The positive control: 24 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped, 123 s, exit code 0, 44 files. Rounds 2 to 4 circled one theme — what a reused work dir or a malformed input leaves behind — and each found one more path the previous fix had not reached; removing the folder first, before any path can complete, is the structural answer the three point fixes were approaching.
