**Round 5 · 2026-09-05: no findings on commit `94159aa`, review loop closed.** Five rounds, six findings: five accepted and fixed, each with a negative test that reproduces the finding and a positive control, and one rejected with evidence — the `whoami` cast — which a person should confirm before the merge. Workflow run 33937175542 on that commit: 15 cases, 0 failed, 28 s. Every finding was on the two new scripts; the runner's three changes and the shipped package drew none. What the loop added over the measurements: five inputs the green runs never had — an unwritable bundle folder, a malformed manifest, a reused work dir in three shapes — which is what a reviewer is for, and what the checklist's own scenarios will keep producing on the machines of #19.

**Not in this change.** The VM runs themselves — the checklist and the bundle are what they will produce. Nothing under `healthcheck/` changes but this record; the acceptance ran the published asset, not the checkout. The root-launcher cases assert the window, the entry point and the report but not the result set, which the `gui` step asserted minutes earlier on the same machine through the language folders' launchers. The wrapper extracts with `Expand-Archive`, which does not propagate the Mark of the Web to the files as Explorer does — the marked path is checklist item M2, and the probe records which one a bundle came from.

## Continuous integration · 2026-09-05 — backlog #20 (`.github/workflows/`; the shipped scripts untouched)

**Change** (PR #9, closes backlog #20). Until now every "the chain passed" in this record was a local run pasted in by hand, and nothing prevented a change from being merged without one: the repository had no `.github/` directory. `.github/workflows/validation-chain.yml` runs the deterministic part of the chain on GitHub's `windows-latest` runner for every push to `main`, every pull request and on request (`workflow_dispatch`) — `tests\Invoke-ValidationChain.ps1 -Steps parse,validator,guards,unit,report,envguard,gui-headless -Package -SkipGui`, the command line `tests/README.md` documents, in Windows PowerShell 5.1 started from `cmd` (the tool's own runtime; a Windows runner's default shell is PowerShell 7, which the tool never runs on), on a checkout with full history (`fetch-depth: 0`, because `selftest_guards.ps1` reads the v1.2.0 anchors from commit `f7c45a9`) and with CPython from `actions/setup-python` for the validator and the asset builder. The chain's exit code is the number of failed cases, so a red run is a failed case, not a broken workflow. The table the chain prints goes to the run's page as the job summary; `summary.md`, every `<step>_<case>.log` and the ZIP the `package` step built are kept as an artifact for 30 days. The workflow asks for `contents: read` only, runs one instance per ref (a newer push cancels the run in progress) and has no path filter on purpose: a required status check that is skipped for a docs-only commit blocks the merge, and a run costs under a minute.

**What runs there and what does not.** Eight steps, because their result depends on the files alone: the parser, the 62-check validator, the two AST guards with their 242-case self-test, the 163 helper unit tests per language, the 103 report-stage tests per language (the IT panel among them, built from real WinForms controls without a window), the 13 language-mode-guard cases per language (a session tightens its own language mode, so a hosted runner is as good as any machine), the headless `Initialize-Gui` smoke test of both entries in both languages, and the asset round trip — build from the tracked files, extract, run the validator from inside the package. Three steps stay local, and `-SkipGui` drops the window part of `package` with them. The real-window `gui` step drives the shipped launchers through UI Automation and needs an interactive desktop, and each IT window samples TCP retransmissions for 125 s. `acceptance` and `resultset` assert every row of a report against the machine's own facts — its gateways, DNS servers, wireless interfaces and counter classes, read the way the script reads them — and on a hosted runner nobody controls those facts; `-RequireHealthy` cannot be asked of a machine whose network is not the point. So a green run means that everything a machine can check unattended still passes on a second, clean Windows machine. It is not the release record: that remains the `-Package -RequireHealthy` run on the reference machine, and #20 named its own limit — the stale function count in the guides would not have been caught, because no check covers it. What it does catch is the class of "changed the code, did not re-run the chain".

**First run — GitHub Actions run 33928171260, the `pull_request` event of PR #9 (merge commit `5dabe60`), 2026-09-05 07:05 UTC+8: 15 cases, 0 failed; 27 s for the chain, 43 s for the job, 48 s from trigger to completion.** The runner reported itself as Microsoft Windows NT 10.0.26100.0 (the Windows Server 2025 image), Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100.33296, CPython 3.14.7. Parser 0 × 2; validator 62 / 62; guards ALL SELF-TESTS OK with the anchors read from `f7c45a9` (so the full-history checkout does what it is there for); unit 163 × 2; report-stage 103 × 2; language-mode guard 13 × 2; headless GUI 4 / 4; package: the asset builds from the tracked files, 223 590 bytes, and the validator run from inside the extracted package is 62 / 62 — every count the same as the pre-flight of the same selection on the reference machine (Windows 11 10.0.26200, Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100.9168, Python 3.14.4: 15 cases, 0 failed, 28 s). Two things differ, and both are measurements rather than defects. The `gui-headless` forms are 940 × 680 on the runner for both entries, where the reference machine shows 940 × 700 (user) and 940 × 872 (IT): `Initialize-Gui` clamps the form to the primary screen's working area minus 40 px, so the runner's desktop is 720 px tall and the IT layout is 192 px shorter there than on the author's screen — the panel's values are unchanged (PingCount 4 / max 20, SampleSeconds 8 / max 120), and it is a reminder that the shipped layout has been looked at on one screen size only (backlog #19). And the ZIP's SHA256 is `c7d52700…` on the runner against `38056e15…` locally for the same 223 590 bytes: `build_asset.py` stamps every entry with the build time, so the digest identifies a build, not a content — the digest in the release notes is that of the asset actually uploaded, and a byte-identical build is backlog #21. The line-ending point of backlog #15 is proven on a machine other than the author's for the first time: on the runner's fresh clone `.gitattributes` alone decides the endings of `healthcheck/**` (`eol=crlf` overrides any `core.autocrlf`), and the validator's CRLF checks and the SHA256 manifest passed both on the checkout and inside the ZIP built from it.

**Not in this change.** Nothing under `healthcheck/` changes but this record; the two shipped scripts, the launchers, the configurations and the validator are byte-identical to v1.2.2. The check is not yet required for merging into `main` — branch protection is a repository setting, left to the owner. Windows 10, a locale opposite to the report language and a machine without a wireless adapter remain backlog #19: `windows-latest` is a Windows Server image and stands in for none of them.

**Second run — 33928421364, on the commit that added this entry (`f702288`): 15 cases, 0 failed, 47 s from trigger to completion,** every count equal to the first. The record itself is a tracked file under `healthcheck/`, so it travels in the ZIP the `package` step builds and the run is the proof that editing it breaks nothing the validator checks.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #9, round 1 · 2026-09-05: no findings on commit `f702288`.** The change is confined to `.github/` and documentation, so the shipped scripts give the reviewer nothing to disagree about, and the workflow's behaviour had been measured twice before the review rather than claimed.

**Round 2 · 2026-09-05, on `79daf19` (the commit that recorded round 1): one P2 finding, accepted.** The artifact was named `validation-chain-<run_number>`, and `run_number` does not change when a run is re-run: `actions/upload-artifact@v4` treats a run's artifacts as immutable and keeps the first attempt's, so the second attempt's upload would have been refused with a conflict and left a job red whose chain had just passed — precisely on the occasion a re-run exists for. The name now carries the attempt (`validation-chain-<run_number>.<run_attempt>`), which is the better of the two remedies the finding offered: `overwrite: true` would have destroyed the evidence of the failed attempt, and the point of keeping logs is that a failure can be read next to the run that followed it. The scenario is not one the three green runs could have exercised — none was re-run — which is the kind of finding a reviewer adds over a measurement: a path the measurements never took.

**Round 3 · 2026-09-05: no findings on commit `3491bfb`, review loop closed.** Three passes, one finding, accepted and fixed. Workflow run 33929572021 on that commit: 15 cases, 0 failed, 28 s for the chain, 51 s from trigger to completion, and the artifact is the first named with its attempt (`validation-chain-4.1`). Four runs of the workflow on four commits of this pull request, every count identical; what changed between them was the record and the artifact name, never a result.

## v1.2.2 — the environment explains itself · 2026-09-04 — backlog #18 (and #14, released here)

**The defect, measured.** Application control (WDAC, AppLocker) can restrict PowerShell to a language mode in which a script may not create .NET objects. Forced into `ConstrainedLanguage` on this machine, every call this tool depends on is refused:

```
New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList        BLOCKED   Cannot create type. Only core types are supported in this language mode.
New-Object System.Net.Sockets.TcpClient        BLOCKED
[System.Net.Dns]::GetHostAddressesAsync(...)   BLOCKED   Method invocation is supported only on core types in this language mode.
Add-Type -AssemblyName System.Windows.Forms    BLOCKED   Definition of new types is not supported in this language mode.
[math]::Round(1.5, 0)                          BLOCKED
Get-NetIPConfiguration                         BLOCKED   the NetTCPIP module cannot be loaded
Set-Content / Get-Date / $env: / Get-CimInstance / ConvertFrom-Json    OK
```

The first of those is the script's own line 50 (`$script:Results = New-Object System.Collections.ArrayList`), so the tool dies before any check, before the report machinery exists, and before it can write even an emergency report. What the user sees is the engine's `Cannot create type. Only core types are supported in this language mode.` — accurate, and useless to the person holding the laptop.

**Change.** A guard ahead of everything else. It reads `$ExecutionContext.SessionState.LanguageMode`, and when that is not `FullLanguage` it explains the situation in the report's language, writes a plain-text environment report and exits with code 3, which the launcher turns into its own message and a pause. The last row of the table above is why this is possible at all: cmdlets still work in a restricted mode, so `Set-Content` can write a file even where `New-Object` cannot make one. The guard and `Write-EnvironmentReport` therefore use nothing but cmdlets, operators and property reads — no .NET types, no `New-Object` — and each optional fact (operating system, culture, PowerShell version) is wrapped on its own, because a locked-down machine may refuse any one of them and a missing line must not cost the report the lines that worked.

```
Network Health Check - environment report
The diagnostic could not run on this computer. Nothing was changed.

Reason: PowerShell is restricted to 'ConstrainedLanguage' language mode by an application-control policy, ...
Tool version: 1.2.2      Computer: ...      PowerShell: 5.1.26100.9168 (Desktop)
Language mode: ConstrainedLanguage           Culture: zh-TW / UI culture: en-US
Operating system: Microsoft Windows 11 Pro

What IT can do:
- Allow NetworkHealthCheck.ps1 in the application-control policy (WDAC / AppLocker), or
- run the check on a computer that is not restricted in this way.
Send this file with the support request.
```

Two smaller pieces travel with it. The report's `PowerShell Version` row now carries the language mode in its details, so a report is evidence that the machine was not restricted — without adding a row, because a row that can only ever say `FullLanguage` (the guard exits otherwise) would be a tautology, and the result-set assertion would have grown for nothing. And `Test-IsRunningFromArchive` warns when the tool is started from inside a compressed folder: Windows opens a ZIP in a temporary view such as `%TEMP%\Temp1_NetworkHealthCheck-1.2.2.zip\...`, where a double-click appears to work but the reports land in a folder that disappears with the view — the evidence the user was told to send to IT is gone by the time they look for it. It reuses the existing startup-message mechanism, so it produces a row only when it fires.

**What was proposed and dropped.** A Mark-of-the-Web check was in the plan and is not here: the launchers run PowerShell with a process-scoped `-ExecutionPolicy Bypass`, so if the script is running at all, the mark did not stop it — the check would report a condition that by construction had no effect. And the silent GUI fallback turned out not to be silent: `Initialize-Gui` has recorded its exception as a startup message since before this work, and startup messages already become `WARN` rows in the report. Verifying that before "fixing" it saved a change that would have duplicated an existing one.

**Tests.** `tests/env_guard_check.ps1` runs the shipped script for real in a restricted mode — a session can tighten its own language mode, so no locked-down machine is needed — and asserts exit code 3, that the run named the mode, that no report was produced, and that exactly one environment report was written naming the mode, the version and the machine. It runs from a copy, so the checkout stays clean, and its assertions are language-independent; the chain runs it as the `envguard` step for both languages (8 cases each). This is a simulation: a real WDAC lockdown also changes module loading and trust, so what it proves is that the guard fires, explains itself and leaves evidence — not that every policy configuration behaves identically. A machine actually under policy remains the gold standard, and is backlog #19. One part of the path is not simulated at all: the launcher turns a nonzero exit code into its own message and a pause, but a `.cmd` starts a fresh PowerShell process, which begins at `FullLanguage` again unless a policy says otherwise — so what the case verifies is the script's own behaviour, and the launcher's handling of exit code 3 rests on the same code path every other nonzero exit already takes. Helper unit tests 156 → 163 per language, the seven new ones covering the compressed-folder check (an extracted folder, the Windows temporary view, forward slashes, a folder merely named `zipped`, the archive itself, an empty path, `$null`).

**Version.** The tool, the packaged validator (`TOOL_VERSION`, `FUNCTION_COUNT` 79 → 81), `README_BILINGUAL.md`, both user READMEs, both technical guides in all three of their locations and the repository's own README now say **1.2.2**; `SHA256SUMS.txt` is regenerated. This release carries backlog #14 as well, which landed in PR #7 under the previous version number.

**Chain run from the branch (Windows 11 10.0.26200, Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100, Python 3.14.4) — `Invoke-ValidationChain.ps1 -Steps parse,validator,guards,unit,report,envguard,gui-headless,acceptance,resultset,package -SkipGui`: 20 cases, 0 failed, 104 s.** Parser 0 × 2; validator 62 / 62; guard self-test ALL SELF-TESTS OK; unit 163 × 2; report-stage 103 × 2; **envguard 8 × 2**; headless GUI 4 / 4; acceptance 4 / 4 (28 / 30 / 28 / 31 rows), all four Overall Healthy; result-set self-check 41 / 41; package: `NetworkHealthCheck-1.2.2.zip`, 221 500 bytes, SHA256 `6ce2727f9657989b7b93e8977ab087ed2120ba139a8cf24e40ffcfad37cdaca7`, and the validator run from inside the extracted package 62 / 62.

**Release validation — `Invoke-ValidationChain.ps1 -Package -RequireHealthy` on commit `91b4373`: 28 cases, 0 failed, 486 s.** The full chain including the real-window steps, which the 1.2.0 rule requires because both shipped scripts changed. Parser 0 × 2; validator 62 / 62; guard self-test OK; unit 163 × 2; report-stage 103 × 2; envguard 13 × 2; headless GUI 4 / 4; **real window 4 / 4** — both entries in both languages through the shipped launchers, titles carrying the new version (`Network Health Check Tool 1.2.2 - IT`, `網路健檢工具 1.2.2（IT）`), the IT runs idle until Start was clicked and keeping the staged 30 pings / 125 s unclamped, all four Overall Healthy with 28 rows, each closed through its own Close button; acceptance 4 / 4; result-set self-check 41 / 41; **package** `NetworkHealthCheck-1.2.2.zip`, 223 107 bytes, SHA256 `c4c8467488d55573feb5ed4d1c74282f267471e78ff5a62414aee877fc00b934`, the validator run from inside the extracted package 62 / 62, and **four more real windows from the extracted package** — the root `Start-English.cmd` and `Start-Traditional-Chinese.cmd` plus each language's `Start-NetworkCheck-IT.cmd`.

No measurement noise this time: every verdict was Healthy under `-RequireHealthy`. The one case that ended otherwise is the unreachable-targets acceptance run — Attention Required with fingerprint `quality`, which is the tool doing its job against targets chosen to fail, and which passes because that case asserts its rows and run options rather than its verdict. It ended Healthy on an earlier run and Attention Required on this one; not asserting the verdict there was the right call.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #8, round 1 · 2026-09-04. One P2 finding on commit `695d210`, accepted.**

*The environment report could be written inside the view it exists to survive.* When a restricted-mode run starts from the Windows compressed-folder view, `$script:BaseDirectory` is the `Temp1_*.zip\...` folder — writable, so the writer succeeded there and returned without ever trying `%TEMP%`; and because the guard exits first, the archive warning further down never runs. Closing the ZIP window would then take the only evidence of the run with it. All four premises check out, and the combination is not exotic: a machine under application control is exactly where someone double-clicks straight out of the downloaded ZIP.

Fixed by moving `Test-IsRunningFromArchive` above the guard — PowerShell executes function definitions in order, so a function defined 250 lines below cannot be called from the guard — and skipping a disposable base directory, with one line of output telling the user to extract the ZIP first. `env_guard_check.ps1` gained the scenario: a copy under a real folder named `Temp1_NetworkHealthCheck.zip`, asserting that nothing is written inside the view, that the reported path is not inside it, and that exactly one report appears in the temporary folder instead (8 → 13 cases per language). Two of its own assertions were corrected while writing it: "the reported path mentions `%TEMP%`" proves nothing when the work dir is itself under `%TEMP%`, and the zh-TW word for a compressed file, written straight into the file, made it non-ASCII — the trap `tests/README.md` documents, since Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads a script without a byte-order mark in the system code page. It is spelled as character codes now.

Two notes for the loop itself. The first attempt at this review failed on Codex's side — `Provided git ref 695d210… does not exist`, minutes after the push, with the commit on the remote and named by the API as the PR's head — and a second trigger produced the review, so a failed review is an outage rather than a result. And Codex does not review automatically here: PR #8 was opened and left alone for five minutes with no comment, no review and no finding, while an explicit `@codex review` produced one within about fifteen seconds.

**Round 2 · 2026-09-04: no findings on commit `e282c37`, review loop closed.** One round, one finding, accepted — and the finding was about the interaction between the two features this change adds rather than about either of them alone, which is the kind a reviewer is good at and a test suite written alongside the code is not.

## Network errors classified by their code · 2026-09-04 — backlog #14 (a `Cause:` line in the report language)

**The defect, reproduced on the reference machine.** Windows PowerShell 5.1 reports `Get-UICulture` as en-US and `Get-Culture` as zh-TW here, and the two disagree in a way that matters: a `SocketException` **constructed** in the session carries .NET's own English text ("No such host is known"), while a **real** DNS failure carries the operating system's text — `System.Net.Sockets.SocketException: '無法識別這台主機。' SocketErrorCode=HostNotFound`. .NET resource strings follow the UI culture; Winsock messages come from the operating system and follow its locale. So the message text can appear in either language on the same machine, cannot be matched against, and must not be the thing the report explains. The code does not move: `SocketException.SocketErrorCode` and `WebException.Status` are enumerations.

**Change.** `Get-NetworkErrorCauseText` walks the exception chain — a failed method call arrives as a `MethodInvocationException`, a task as an `AggregateException`, a ping as a `PingException`, so the socket error sits two or three levels down — and returns one sentence in the report's language followed by the code: `The name could not be resolved (no DNS record). [SocketError HostNotFound]`. `Add-NetworkErrorCause` puts that line above the original text, never instead of it. 23 codes are mapped in both languages: 12 `SocketError` (HostNotFound, TryAgain, NoData, TimedOut, ConnectionRefused, NetworkUnreachable, HostUnreachable, ConnectionReset, ConnectionAborted, NetworkDown, AddressNotAvailable, AccessDenied) and 11 `WebExceptionStatus` (Timeout, NameResolutionFailure, ProxyNameResolutionFailure, ConnectFailure, TrustFailure, SecureChannelFailure, ReceiveFailure, SendFailure, ConnectionClosed, ServerProtocolViolation, RequestProhibitedByProxy). An unmapped code yields `[SocketError <Code>]` on its own — no invented sentence, but a locale-independent name the reader can look up. `WebExceptionStatus.ProtocolError` is deliberately not mapped: a non-success HTTP status is not a failure path here, and the script already reports it through the response.

Six call sites carry the line: the exception details of every check (`Get-ExceptionDetails`, so a failed DNS lookup, a step error and the emergency report all gain it), the per-attempt ping log, the TCP failure text, both HTTP failure paths and a traceroute hop status. Errors raised by cmdlets, CIM/WMI or the file system have no such code, keep the operating system's wording unchanged, and §5 of both technical guides now says so.

What the en-US report carries after the change, from the acceptance run against unreachable targets on this machine (the wrapper message that used to be the whole story is still there, above it the cause, and where the operating system spoke Chinese its words are kept underneath):

```
[INFO] Extra ping: nhc-no-such-host.invalid
    Attempt 1: error, Exception calling "Send" with "2" argument(s): "An exception occurred during a Ping
    request." | Cause: The name could not be resolved (no DNS record). [SocketError HostNotFound]
[INFO] Extra URL
    Cause: The host name in the URL could not be resolved. [WebExceptionStatus NameResolutionFailure]
```

The TCP target of the same run (`192.0.2.1:9`) carries no cause line, correctly: it ends in the script's own timeout, whose message is already written in the report language.

**Tests.** The helper unit tests go from 89 to 152 per language. The expected sentences differ per language, so the assertions check language-independent properties: every mapped code produces a sentence followed by its code name, an unmapped code produces the code alone, the chain is followed through `AggregateException` and `PingException`, a non-network exception and `$null` produce nothing, a chain deeper than the walk produces nothing (the depth is bounded, so a self-referencing chain cannot loop), the original text is never dropped, and `Get-ExceptionDetails` puts the cause on the first line and only there.

One assertion is new in kind: **both language files must carry the same table keys.** The validator's dual-language skeleton comparison cannot see a misspelled key — measured on the shipped `strip_ps`, `@{'HostNotFound' = '...'}` and `@{'HostNotFnud' = '...'}` both reduce to `@{<STR>=<STR>}`, identical, while a *missing* entry does change the token count and is caught. The new test reads the peer language's file by AST, collects the hashtable keys of `Get-NetworkErrorCauseText` and requires the same set on both sides plus the full canonical list. Verified by mutation: with `HostUnreachable` misspelled in a staged copy of the zh-TW file, the case fails and names the key. `FUNCTION_COUNT` in the validator goes 77 → 79.

**A run that reaches the new code.** On a healthy machine no acceptance run produces a network error, so the wiring would have shipped unexercised — the 1.2.0 lesson in a different costume. The acceptance step gained a fourth case: `-PingTarget nhc-no-such-host.invalid -TcpTarget 192.0.2.1:9 -HttpUrl https://nhc-no-such-host.invalid/` (RFC 2606 `.invalid`, RFC 5737 TEST-NET-1), which exercises the ping, TCP and HTTP failure paths on every run of the chain and requires at least one result row to carry a `[SocketError ...]` or `[WebExceptionStatus ...]` cause. Which code appears depends on the network — a resolver that answers for an unknown name turns a name failure into a connect failure — so any code counts. The verdict is not asserted for that case: targets given at launch are optional by design, so on this machine the three failures were reported as INFO and the run still ended Overall Healthy, while a configuration that makes them required would legitimately end otherwise. Everything else about the case - exit code, run options, extra targets, the complete result set (31 rows) - is asserted as usual.

**Not in this change.** The tool version stays 1.2.1: the bump to 1.2.2, the environment checks of backlog #18 and the release come next. `healthcheck/` now differs from the published 1.2.1 asset in nine files (both scripts, the six guide copies, the validator's function count) plus the regenerated `SHA256SUMS.txt`.

**Chain run from the branch (Windows 11 10.0.26200, Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100, Python 3.14.4) — `Invoke-ValidationChain.ps1 -Steps parse,validator,guards,unit,report,gui-headless,acceptance,resultset,package -SkipGui`: 18 cases, 0 failed, 85 s.** Parser 0 × 2; validator 62 / 62; guard self-test ALL SELF-TESTS OK; unit **152 × 2**; report-stage 103 × 2; headless GUI 4 / 4; acceptance 4 / 4 with 28 / 30 / 28 / 31 rows, the new case classifying its ping and HTTP failures by code; result-set self-check 41 / 41; package: the asset builds from the 25 tracked files and the validator run from inside the extracted package is 62 / 62. The en-US user console run ended Overall Problem Detected with fingerprint `quality` — TCPv4 retransmissions above the threshold during its 8-second sample on this Wi-Fi, a measurement rather than a defect, and `-RequireHealthy` was off. A later documentation edit (the function count in both guides, 77 → 79) was followed by regenerating `SHA256SUMS.txt` and re-running `-Steps validator,package`: 2 / 2, asset SHA256 `1534f1c651b16363eda9b9dfd6888571f05d0d33c32daeb1b6e8eceeaf79d566`.

**The real-window steps are still owed.** This change touches both shipped scripts, so `gui` and the window part of `package` must run before the release — the 1.2.0 rule. They were not part of this run.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #7, round 1 · 2026-09-04. Two P2 findings on commit `ddfbe8a`, both accepted and fixed, and both the same defect in two places.**

1. *The per-attempt ping line put the cause after the operating system's message.* The rule this change states is that the explanation comes first; the attempt line appended it instead, so a reader still had to get past a sentence in another language before reaching the part written for them.
2. *The traceroute hop replaced the message with the cause.* Worse than the first: `Exception.Message` was discarded outright, against the "never instead of it" rule the same change states.

Both sites had the rule hand-written into them, while `Add-NetworkErrorCause` — used by the TCP and both HTTP paths — implemented it properly; the two hand-written copies drifted. The fix is therefore not two patched lines but one decision point: `Add-NetworkErrorCause -SingleLine` returns `Cause: <cause> | <original>` for the two places whose output is one line, the same function keeps the two-line shape everywhere else, and both single-line sites now call it. Four unit assertions cover the switch (one line, original kept, cause before the original, no cause leaves the text alone), so the drift cannot come back unnoticed; §5 of both guides now states the order and where each shape applies. Helper unit tests 152 → 156 per language.

Re-validation after round 1 — `-Steps parse,validator,guards,unit,report,gui-headless,acceptance,resultset,package -SkipGui`: 18 cases, 0 failed, 84 s. Parser 0 × 2; validator 62 / 62; guard self-test OK; unit 156 × 2; report-stage 103 × 2; headless GUI 4 / 4; acceptance 4 / 4 (28 / 30 / 28 / 31 rows), all four Overall Healthy this time; result-set self-check 41 / 41; package: validator inside the extracted package 62 / 62. The en-US report of the unreachable-targets case now reads `Attempt 1: error, Cause: The name could not be resolved (no DNS record). [SocketError HostNotFound] | Exception calling "Send" ...` — the explanation first, the operating system's own words after it.

**Round 2 · 2026-09-04: no findings on commit `0f8ebc3`, review loop closed.** Two rounds, two findings, both accepted and both traced to one cause rather than patched where they showed.

## Guards on the PowerShell AST · 2026-09-04 — backlog #17 (`tests/`; the packaged validator drops from 66 to 62 checks)

**Change** (closes backlog #17). The two guards that keep the v1.2.0 GUI regressions out of the shipped scripts were regular expressions over the comment-free logical lines of the file, inside the packaged `tools/validate_release.py`. They now run on the PowerShell AST in the test chain: `tests/ast_guards.ps1`, with `tests/selftest_guards.ps1` as the acceptance set. Nineteen of the twenty-one Codex rounds on PR #4 had gone into teaching the patterns another spelling of the same two mistakes; the parser answers all of them at once.

- **`Find-UnparenthesizedArithmetic`** — the comma binds tighter than every arithmetic operator, so `New-Object System.Drawing.Point(22, 84 + $offset)` really is `Point(@(22, 84) + $offset)`: one three-element argument, the runtime's "argument count 3", and in v1.2.0 an `Initialize-Gui` that threw and sent both entry points to console mode. Two signatures, one per spelling. A parenthesized or `@( )` argument list whose top-level expression is an arithmetic `BinaryExpressionAst` over the `ArrayLiteralAst` the commas built — `(22, (84 + $offset))` is an array literal, `(780, [math]::Min(560 + $offset, $h))` keeps its arithmetic inside a method call, and `(1 + 2)` or `(@($a) + $b)` have no comma-built array on the left, so none of them matches. And, for `-ArgumentList 22, 84 + $offset`, a bare-word arithmetic operator among the command's elements on a call `StaticParameterBinder` cannot satisfy: the comma ends the array and the operator reaches New-Object as a positional argument it has no parameter for.
- **`Find-OverwrittenParameter`** — a script's top-level scope and its `$script:` scope are the same variable table, so v1.2.0's `$script:Interactive = $false` overwrote the bound `-Interactive` and the IT launcher opened the user layout. Every write that reaches that table is read from the AST: assignments (compound and multiple ones included), `++` / `--`, a `foreach` loop variable, `Set-Variable` / `New-Variable` / `Clear-Variable` / `Remove-Variable`, and the item cmdlets on the `Variable:` drive. `$script:` / `$global:` counts anywhere, unqualified / `$local:` / `$private:` only outside a function (`VariablePath` and the parent chain say which), and the only accepted initializer is the parameter's own value — `$Interactive`, `[bool]$Interactive`, `@($PingTarget)`, `$Interactive.IsPresent` and nestings of those — because a transformed value belongs in a variable of its own. `StaticParameterBinder` resolves abbreviated parameter names, positional and quoted arguments and the cmdlets' aliases, which the session itself supplies (`Get-Alias -Definition`), so `sv`, `set`, `nv`, `clv`, `rv`, `si`, `sc`, `ni`, `cli`, `ri`, `rm`, `del`, `erase`, `rd` and `rmdir` need no pattern each.

The rewrite closes the three findings assessed and deferred in round 21 of PR #4 — the multiple assignment `$tmp, $script:Interactive = 1, $false`, `Clear-Variable` / `Remove-Variable`, and an alias of `New-Object` (Windows PowerShell 5.1 defines none, but one defined in the session resolves like any other) — and adds what the AST makes free: a `foreach` loop variable, a module-qualified call, `@(22, 84 + $offset)` as an argument list, an arithmetic spine longer than one operator, and here-strings, comments and string data that the text stripper had to recognise by hand and the parser simply knows. `$env:` and other drive-qualified names are excluded as a different store, `[System.Drawing.Point]::new(22, 84 + $offset)` is not flagged because a method call splits its own arguments on the commas, and the comma mistake outside a `New-Object` argument list stays out of scope: there it is legitimate array concatenation. Out of scope by design in both guards, as before: code the parser cannot see through — `Invoke-Expression`, a command name or a variable path built at runtime, splatting. A `-Scope` passed as a variable is now flagged rather than trusted, and a script block passed to a command (`& { }`, `ForEach-Object { }`) is treated as its enclosing scope, since `ForEach-Object` really does write the caller's variables and flagging is a guard's safe direction.

**Acceptance** (`tests/selftest_guards.ps1`, the `guards` step of the chain). The v1.2.0 files, read from git at commit `f7c45a9`, must be flagged at line 77 (en-US) / 70 (zh-TW) and at the six constructor lines, and the shipped 1.2.1 files must parse and come back clean — the two anchors backlog #17 named. The corpus is the one built up over the PR #4 rounds, one case per spelling a round proposed: **every classification recorded before the rewrite is unchanged**, and 28 cases were added for the shapes above (152 top-level, 33 in-function, 57 constructor cases; the old set was 133 / 26 / 46, where the 47th constructor case was a duplicate key the Python dictionary had silently collapsed). Two things came out of running it. First, one recorded case — `New-Object System.Drawing.Point(22, 84) <# x + y #> $b = 1` — parses as a New-Object call with three stray arguments, so "the binder reports an exception" alone would have flagged it for a reason that has nothing to do with arithmetic; the signature therefore requires a **bare-word arithmetic operator** among the elements as well, which is the mistake itself and keeps the case clean as recorded. Second, the self-test's first run failed on `$hits.Add('{0} (${1})' -f $line, $name)` in the new guard: inside a method call the comma separates arguments, so the format string was handed one value instead of two — the same comma-precedence mistake the guard exists for, caught by the guard's own test set before it left the working tree.

**The shipped package.** `tools/validate_release.py` loses the two guards and their text machinery (`strip_block_comments`, `strip_line_comment`, `abbr`, `logical_lines`) and runs **62 checks instead of 66** — the file is cross-platform and the guards need Windows PowerShell. One clause of the technical guide's 1.2.1 paragraph said that the validator rejects both patterns; it now names where the guard lives and the new check count, in both languages (six files, because each guide is packaged three times). `SHA256SUMS.txt` was regenerated for those seven files and matches. The two language scripts, the launchers, the configurations and the READMEs are byte-identical to v1.2.1, the tool version is unchanged, and nothing the tool does at run time changed — but the package is no longer byte-identical to the published 1.2.1 asset, and the next release must be built from this tree.

**Chain run from the branch (Windows 11 10.0.26200, Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100, Python 3.14.4) — `Invoke-ValidationChain.ps1 -Steps parse,validator,guards,unit,report,gui-headless,acceptance,resultset,package -SkipGui`: 17 cases, 0 failed, 79 s.** Parser 0 × 2; validator 62 / 62; guard self-test ALL SELF-TESTS OK (152 / 33 / 57 cases, the anchors flagged at 77 / 70 and the six constructor lines, both current files clean); unit 89 × 2; report-stage 103 × 2; headless GUI 4 / 4; acceptance 3 / 3, all three Overall Healthy with 28 / 30 / 28 rows; result-set self-check 41 / 41; package: the asset builds from the 25 tracked files (203 334 bytes, SHA256 `e8caada1f680bb985c4bab6c4098898ce73c2759714855059ff63423e0b524cd`) and the validator run from inside the extracted package is 62 / 62. The real-window steps (`gui`, and the window part of `package`) were not part of this run: the two shipped scripts are byte-identical to v1.2.1 and this change touches neither them nor the runner's window cases. `-Package -RequireHealthy` with the windows remains the release gate.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #6, round 1 · 2026-09-04: no findings, review loop closed.** The pass on commit `eefcd27` reported nothing, and it is the first round of a pull request in this repository to do so (PR #4: 21 passes, 30 findings; PR #5: 13 passes, 30 findings). Three things are different here. The new code is confined to `tests/` and the two shipped scripts are untouched, so there is no shipped behaviour to disagree about. The guards' behaviour is pinned by 242 corpus cases and the two v1.2.0 anchors, so the alternative spellings a review round used to propose are already written down as expectations — the rounds of PR #4 are what built that set. And the parser replaced the class of defect those rounds kept finding: a regular expression over text has to be told about every spelling, while `Parser` and `StaticParameterBinder` know them all, which is the whole point of backlog #17.

## Test chain committed · 2026-09-04 — backlog #16 (`tests/`; no change to the shipped package)

**Change** (PR #5, closes backlog #16). The scripts that produced the 1.2.1 record lived in a session scratchpad; they are now committed under a top-level `tests/` folder, outside the shipped `healthcheck/` package, with one runner that reproduces the whole chain from a fresh checkout:

- `Invoke-ValidationChain.ps1` — runs the steps below in order on staged copies of `healthcheck/<lang>/` under `%TEMP%\nhc-tests\<timestamp>\` (the repository is never written to), keeps every step's raw output as a log, prints the summary table and exits with the number of failed cases. `-Package` adds the release-asset round trip, `-RequireHealthy` makes Overall Healthy a pass condition, `-SkipGui` drops the real-window runs for a session without a desktop, `-Steps` selects steps.
- `parse_check.ps1`, `unit_tests.ps1`, `report_stage_tests.ps1` (was `report_stage_tests_v121.ps1`), `gui_repro.ps1`, `gui_check.ps1` — committed as they ran for 1.2.1: identical text, and identical bytes for all but `gui_repro.ps1`, whose CRLF line endings git stores as LF like every other file.
- `selftest_guards.py` — the self-test of the validator's two guards with the corpus built up over the PR #4 review rounds (133 top-level, 26 in-function and 46 constructor cases); it now resolves paths from the repository root and reads the v1.2.0 anchor files from git (commit f7c45a9) instead of two loose copies. This is the acceptance set of backlog #17.
- `build_asset.py` — the packaging rule (tracked files under `healthcheck/` only, working-tree bytes, one top-level folder, deflate), paths resolved from the repository root.
- `tests/README.md` — each step, its pass condition and the 1.2.1 reference results.

The runner turns into assertions what was previously read by eye from the scripts' output: the JSON report's `EntryPoint`, `PingCount`, `SampleSeconds` and `TracerouteHops` must match the launch (for the IT window run a staged configuration of 30 pings / 125 s must come back unclamped), the IT window title must carry the IT marker and the user title must not, the window must be closed through its own Close button, extra targets given as switches must appear in the run options, and the schema must be 2.

`healthcheck/` is untouched: both language scripts, the launchers, the configurations, the guides and the validator are byte-identical to v1.2.1 (`SHA256SUMS.txt` unchanged). The top-level `README.md` points at `tests/`; `.gitignore` excludes `__pycache__/`.

Every assertion the case exists for held (`EntryPoint = IT`, 4 / 8 unclamped, IT marker in the title, closed through Close, exit 0); `-RequireHealthy` turned the verdict into a failure as designed. The package step was re-run on its own (`-Steps package -RequireHealthy`): 3 / 3, 37 s.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #5, round 1 · 2026-09-04.** Three P2 findings on commit 84f068b, all on `tests/gui_check.ps1`, all accepted and fixed:

1. *The IT window was not verified idle before Start was clicked.* An IT entry that started by itself would have disabled Start, the unconditional `BM_CLICK` would have been ignored, and the auto-produced report would still have satisfied every downstream assertion — the very "no auto-start" invariant the case documents was not enforced. Fix: before the click the script requires Start enabled, no report since launch, and a snapshot of every control (window class, name, enabled state) unchanged over a 3-second hold; after the click it requires the run to start within 10 s (Start disabled or the window changing). The user entry gets the mirror check: the run must start within 10 s with nothing clicked.
2. *A nonzero exit code of the GUI process was logged and then ignored.* Fix: the script exits nonzero when the launched process (or the launcher) does not exit 0, when the window does not close within 15 s of the Close click, or when the launcher wrote `LauncherError.txt`; the runner in turn requires the "closed via the Close button; process exit code 0" line.
3. *The real-window runs bypassed the shipped launchers.* `powershell.exe` was started with reconstructed arguments, so an IT launcher that dropped `-Interactive` or `-ExpandDetails` would still have passed. Fix: `gui_check.ps1 -Via Launcher` (the default) runs `Start-NetworkCheck.cmd` / `Start-NetworkCheck-IT.cmd` through `cmd.exe` from the package folder, finds the window of the PowerShell process the launcher started, and prints the launcher's effective command line; the runner asserts that line carries `-Interactive -ExpandDetails` for the IT entry only, and asserts `ExpandDetails` in the JSON run options (IT true, user false). The console acceptance runs of the user entry now go through `Start-NetworkCheck-Console.cmd` as well (its trailing `pause` reads from NUL); the IT-switches run still calls the script directly, because the launcher takes no arguments. Recorded on the way: through UI Automation the panel's spinner values are readable after all, as the names of the spinners' inner edit controls (`'4' '8' '3'` with the default configuration); the JSON run options stay the evidence.

The one failure was again a measurement, not a defect: the en-US user console run ended Overall Attention Required with fingerprint `quality` because TCPv4 retransmitted 15 of 468 segments (3.2 %, above the 2 % warning threshold) during its 8-second sample; exit code, launcher, run options and report were all as expected, and the acceptance step re-run on its own passed 3 / 3 in 35 s. Parser 0 × 2; validator 66 / 66; guard self-test OK; unit 89 × 2; report-stage 103 × 2; headless GUI 4 / 4; real window through the launchers 4 / 4 — user entries started by themselves, IT entries idle for 3 s and started on the click with `PingCount = 30`, `SampleSeconds = 125`, `ExpandDetails = true` and the command line `-Interactive -ExpandDetails`; acceptance 3 / 3, two of them through `Start-NetworkCheck-Console.cmd`; package: asset SHA256 `bb95fe911f9476ac7770e2ce6741a41fb4d58901c2347154be02036036b609af`, validator inside the extracted package 66 / 66, the extracted `Start-NetworkCheck-IT.cmd` opened the IT window in both languages. The shipped scripts are unchanged.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #5, round 2 · 2026-09-04.** Two P2 findings on commit a1cbc66, both on `tests/Invoke-ValidationChain.ps1`, both accepted and fixed:

1. *The end-to-end runs did not assert the result set.* The window and console cases validated run options, schema and (with `-RequireHealthy`) the verdict, so a `Run-AllChecks` that skipped a diagnostic while still writing a valid report — even an empty, "healthy" one — would have passed; the documented 28 / 30 rows were never checked. Fix: every window and console case now requires exactly the rows its configuration and run options call for: one per configured or extra ping / DNS / TCP / HTTP target (the `AUTO_GATEWAY` target as `ping-gateway`), one connectivity-group row per group, one per enabled IT diagnostic read from the report's own `ChecksEnabled`, the fixed rows (`config-file`, `config`, `environment`, `system`, `adapters`, `gateway-config`, `dns-config`, `expected-standard`, TCPv4 and TCPv6 retransmissions), each in its scope, and nothing else — an unknown tag, a missing row or a total that does not add up fails the case; per-adapter rows must exist and pair up (one counter row per adapter row); extra targets given as switches must have a row naming them. With the shipped configuration on the reference machine that is 28 rows, or 30 with the IT switches. `tests/selftest_resultset.ps1` is the negative self-check of that assertion on a real report: intact it is clean; a removed row, a rogue tag, an IT row moved to the Main scope, a missing extra-target row, the counter rows removed and an empty set are each reported (7 / 7).
2. *The package root's launchers were never run.* `README_BILINGUAL.md` sends people to `Start-English.cmd` / `Start-Traditional-Chinese.cmd`, but the package cases went straight to each language folder's IT launcher, so a broken root launcher would have passed. Fix: `gui_check.ps1 -LauncherPath` runs a given launcher from its own folder (the PowerShell process may be a child or a grandchild of that cmd.exe), and the package step opens the user entry of each language through its root launcher and the IT entry through each language folder's `Start-NetworkCheck-IT.cmd` — four window runs from the extracted package.

Parser 0 × 2; validator 66 / 66; guard self-test OK; unit 89 × 2; report-stage 103 × 2; headless GUI 4 / 4; real window through the launchers 4 / 4 with the result set asserted (28 rows each); acceptance 3 / 3 (28 / 30 / 28 rows, the extra targets' rows present); package: asset SHA256 `9d30a39cd6827a1d2b4a538d898af35dfe36042e9163216c4b4cdd5cdb0e9ac4`, validator inside the extracted package 66 / 66, `Start-English.cmd` and `Start-Traditional-Chinese.cmd` opened the user window and both `Start-NetworkCheck-IT.cmd` the IT window from the extracted package. The shipped scripts are unchanged.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #5, round 3 · 2026-09-04.** Two P2 findings on commit f98bfa4, both on the result-set assertion in `tests/Invoke-ValidationChain.ps1`, both accepted and fixed:

1. *The IT diagnostics to expect were read from the report under test.* `Test-ResultSet` took the enabled diagnostics from the report's own `RunOptions.ChecksEnabled`, so a `Set-RunOptions` that dropped a configured diagnostic in execution and in `ChecksEnabled` alike would have been expected as zero rows and passed. Fix: the expected diagnostics come from the staged configuration (`Checks.*`, with the script's rule that only a boolean true enables a check) and the launch switches (`-NoWifi` / `-NoTraceroute`), and the report's `ChecksEnabled` must agree with them row for row.
2. *`ping-gateway` rows were counted from the configuration placeholder.* `AUTO_GATEWAY` resolves to every distinct IPv4 default gateway of the connected adapters, so a multihomed machine legitimately produces two rows and the expectation of one would have failed every end-to-end case there. Fix: the expected count is one row per distinct IPv4 default gateway of the machine's connected adapters with an IPv4 address, read from the operating system (`Get-NetIPConfiguration`) — one "no target" row when there is none — and, when gateways exist, every `ping-gateway` row must name one of them, each once. `tests/selftest_resultset.ps1` gained the two negative cases (a diagnostic dropped by the run and by `ChecksEnabled` alike; a gateway row naming a non-gateway address): 9 / 9.

Re-validation after round 3 — `Invoke-ValidationChain.ps1 -Package -RequireHealthy`: 24 cases, 1 failed, 437 s. The one failure was a measurement, not a defect: the en-US IT-switches console run (six pings) lost one echo of six to the gateway and to 8.8.8.8 (16.7 % loss, above the 5 % warning threshold) and ended Overall Attention Required with fingerprint `quality`; its 30 rows, run options and extra-target rows were all as expected. The acceptance step re-run on its own warned once more on the gateway (again 5 of 6) and then passed 3 / 3 in 38 s: on a six-ping sample one lost echo is a warning by the tool's own thresholds. **Independent review — Codex, PR #5, round 4 · 2026-09-04.** Two P2 findings on commit 0b62e48, both on `tests/Invoke-ValidationChain.ps1`, both accepted and fixed:

1. *A machine without a usable adapter would have failed every end-to-end case.* With every adapter disabled or disconnected the snapshot is empty and the script writes the aggregate `adapters` failure with no per-adapter rows (and, as the code shows, no `gateway-config` / `dns-config` rows either, while counter rows may still name whatever the counter sample saw); the assertion demanded an adapter row unconditionally, so a real network problem would have failed the chain even with `-RequireHealthy` off — against the documented rule. Fix: the assertion now reads the machine facts the way the script's own first source does (`Get-NetIPConfiguration`: an adapter counts when it is Up and has an IPv4 or IPv6 address; the gateways are those of the adapters with an IPv4 address) and expects one `adapter` row per connected adapter with an address, one counter row per adapter row, and the zero-adapter shape (failed aggregate row, no adapter / gateway / DNS rows, counter rows unconstrained) only when the machine has no connected adapter with an address — so the shape is accepted where it is legitimate and reported where it is a regression. On the way the `expected-standard` expectation became configuration-driven as well: one row per rule category in `Expected` (address or subnet allowlist, prefix lengths, gateways, DNS servers, DHCP mode), one informational row without rules, one failure row without adapters — the shipped configuration has no rules.
2. *`selftest_resultset.ps1` was never run by the chain.* Fix: a `resultset` step (in the default set, after `acceptance`) runs the negative self-check on the acceptance step's en-US user report, or on a report it produces itself through the console launcher when that step was not selected. The self-check grew to 12 cases: the zero-adapter shape with injected machine facts (must be clean) and on this machine (must be reported), and a configuration with two standard rules against a report with one standard row (must be reported); the machine facts are injectable for that purpose.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #5, round 5 · 2026-09-04.** Three P2 findings on commit 1c54829 — the checkpoint round; all three concern the portability of the result-set assertion to machines unlike the reference one, all accepted and fixed:

1. *The gateway negative case of the self-check depended on live connectivity.* On a machine without an IPv4 default gateway the report's `ping-gateway` row is the "no target" row without an address, the tampering changed nothing, and `Test-ResultSet` skips the by-target check when the machine has no gateway — so the case would have come back clean while expecting a mismatch, failing the default chain. Fix: every tampered case of `selftest_resultset.ps1` now runs against injected machine facts (three connected adapters; a synthetic TEST-NET gateway for the gateway cases, which construct the malformed row themselves), and only the intact report is checked against the real facts as the positive control.
2. *A required connectivity group without targets was expected as zero rows.* `Test-ConnectivityTargets` writes one `connectivity-group` row per group named on a TCP or HTTP target or listed in `RequiredConnectivityGroups` — a required group without targets gets its own "no executable items" row — while the assertion derived the groups from the targets alone. Fix: the expected set is the union of both, like the script.
3. *The machine facts ignored the script's CIM fallback.* `Get-MachineFacts` read `Get-NetIPConfiguration` only; where the script falls back to CIM the counts would have been wrong and the `data-source` warning row unexpected. Fix: the facts are read the way `Get-NetworkSnapshot` reads them — the cmdlet first; when it throws, the CIM path plus an expected `data-source` row; when it is missing or returns nothing, the CIM path without the row (every IP-enabled `Win32_NetworkAdapterConfiguration`, with its valid IPv4 default gateways for the entries that have an IPv4 address).

The self-check grew to 17 cases (a required group without targets; the data-source row expected and absent, expected and present; a gateway row naming the machine's gateway; the intact report against injected facts).

**Independent review — Codex, PR #5, round 6 · 2026-09-04.** Three P2 findings on commit ca76f53, all accepted and fixed; two are the same lesson once more — the self-check's fixtures were still derived from the live report and the live machine — and the third is one more legitimate report shape:

1. *The synthetic adapter count was normalised to the live report.* On a machine with no adapter rows the injected facts became zero adapters, and the negative cases about adapters and standard rules would have failed. Fix: the tampered cases now start from a normalised fixture built from the live report — exactly three adapter rows and three counter rows, one gateway row naming a synthetic TEST-NET gateway, gateway and DNS settings rows present, one TCPv4 and one TCPv6 retransmission row, no data-source row, a passing aggregate row — checked against fixed synthetic facts; the live report against the real facts stays the positive control.
2. *A report produced through the CIM fallback already carries a data-source row.* The "absent" case would have been clean and the "present" case a duplicate. Fix: the fixture removes any data-source row; the present case adds exactly one.
3. *Exactly two retransmission rows were expected.* When only one of the TCPv4 / TCPv6 performance-counter classes can be read, `Compare-TcpCounters` writes an error row for the unavailable protocol from each of the two samples plus the result row for the other — three legitimate rows. Fix: the machine facts read the availability of `Win32_PerfRawData_Tcpip_TCPv4` / `TCPv6` the way `Get-TcpCounterSnapshot` does, the expected rows are one per readable protocol and two per unreadable one, and each protocol must be named by at least one row. The self-check covers a report with TCPv6 unreadable (accepted with matching facts, reported with both readable) and a report that names TCPv4 twice and TCPv6 never.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #5, round 7 · 2026-09-04.** Three P2 findings on commit 40a9cd8, all accepted and fixed — two more legitimate report shapes the result-set assertion did not model, and a stale-report hazard in the self-check step:

1. *Neither TCP counter class readable.* The baseline step throws, `Invoke-CheckStep` records a `step-error` row, and `Compare-TcpCounters` gets a null sample and writes the single generic "System Counters" row — the assertion expected four protocol rows, rejected `step-error` as unknown and looked for both protocol names. Fix: the machine facts distinguish that case, and the expected shape is then one `step-error` row(s) plus one generic `tcp-retransmissions` row, with the per-protocol naming skipped.
2. *Adapter statistics unavailable.* When `Get-NetAdapterStatistics` cannot be used, both sampling steps end as `step-error` rows and the analysis step writes one aggregate "Before/After Comparison" `adapter-errors` row — no counter row per adapter. Fix: the facts probe the cmdlet the way `Get-AdapterStatisticsSnapshot` does, and the expected shape is then two `step-error` rows plus one aggregate row. `step-error` is otherwise still an unexpected tag, so any other step failure keeps failing the case.
3. *The self-check could pick a stale report.* With a reused `-WorkDir` the oldest JSON in the staged `Reports` folder could belong to an earlier invocation, and the `resultset` step only checked that some JSON existed. Fix: the acceptance step records the path of the en-US user report it produced, the `resultset` step passes exactly that file (or the one it produces itself, selected by start time), and `selftest_resultset.ps1` takes `-ReportPath`; `-ReportDir` on its own now selects the newest user-entry report and is kept for manual use only.

The self-check grew to 24 cases: both counter classes unreadable (accepted with matching facts, reported with both readable), adapter statistics unavailable (accepted with matching facts, reported with the cmdlet available), and a step-error row on an otherwise healthy machine.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #5, round 8 · 2026-09-04.** Three P2 findings on commit cb94cc6, all accepted and fixed:

1. *The machine facts did not mirror the WMI branch of the fallback.* `Get-NetworkSnapshotFromCim` and `Get-TcpCounterSnapshot` use `Get-CimInstance` when it exists and `Get-WmiObject` otherwise; the facts called `Get-CimInstance` unconditionally, which under the runner's stop-on-error setting would have thrown on a machine with WMI only. Fix: the facts select CIM or WMI the same way (a `Get-CimOrWmiInstance` helper like the script's), for the adapter fallback and for the TCP counter probe alike, and a machine with neither yields the zero-adapter facts the script would also end up with.
2. *`AUTO_DNS` ping targets expand to one row per DNS server.* `Resolve-PingTargets` turns the documented `AUTO_DNS` placeholder into every distinct DNS server of the primary adapters (one `ping-target` row each, or one "no target" row), while the assertion counted every non-gateway ping target as one row. Fix: the facts carry the DNS servers of the primary adapters (the adapters with an IPv4 address and a gateway, or all with an IPv4 address when none has a gateway — `Get-PrimaryAdapters`), and each `AUTO_DNS` entry expects one row per server. The shipped configuration uses no `AUTO_DNS` target; the self-check covers it with a configuration tweak and injected DNS facts.
3. *Extra targets were checked for containment only.* The IT-switches case accepted a run that duplicated a requested target or added an unrequested one, and the result-set assertion took its extra-target counts from that same report. Fix: `ExtraTargets.Ping / Dns / Tcp / Http` in the report must equal the launch exactly — empty for the runs without switches — before the rows are counted, so the counts now rest on the launch, not on the report.

The self-check grew to 26 cases (an `AUTO_DNS` target against two DNS servers, expected as two rows and reported as one).

**Review loop summary, PR #5:** 13 Codex passes, 30 findings — all accepted and fixed in 12 rounds, none rejected, none deferred. Three findings (round 1) concerned the strength of the window driver itself: the shipped launchers were not exercised, the IT window was not verified idle before Start, and exit codes were not propagated. The other 27 concerned the result-set assertion introduced in round 2 — first its existence, then its correctness on machines unlike the reference one: several gateways, DNS servers or wireless interfaces, no connected adapter, the CIM and WMI fallbacks and their step-error rows, unreadable performance counters, unavailable adapter statistics, required-only connectivity groups, configured standard rules, `AUTO_DNS`, exact extra targets, and state that moves during a two-minute run. Each shape is now derived from the configuration, the launch and machine facts read before and after the run, and each has a negative case in `tests/selftest_resultset.ps1` (41 cases). Every round re-ran the whole chain; the runs that were not all green (the first run and rounds 1, 3, 7, 9, 10 and 11) failed only on the verdict of a window or console run while the Wi-Fi carried real retransmissions or lost echoes, and every structural check held in every run. **`healthcheck/` is unchanged throughout: the shipped scripts, launchers, configurations, guides and validator are byte-identical to v1.2.1 and `SHA256SUMS.txt` is untouched.**

## v1.2.1 · 2026-09-03 — IT panel keeps configured values; two 1.2.0 GUI regressions

**Changes** (PR #4). The first item is the seventh Codex pass on PR #3; the other two were found by the first real GUI runs of 1.2.0 — the earlier chain exercised the WinForms code by review only.

1. **IT panel truncated configured sampling values (Codex, PR #3 round 7 — P2).** `Set-OptionsPanelValues` clamped `PingCount` to the spinner's 1–20 range and `RetransmissionSampleSeconds` to 1–120, and Start wrote the clamped values back through `Get-RunOptionsFromPanel` even when nothing was touched, while configuration validation accepts any positive Int32 — so `PingCount: 30` ran as 30 from the user entry and the console but as 20 from the IT launcher (300 s → 120 s likewise). Fix: the spinner's `Maximum` is raised to the configured value before the value is assigned (`max(20, configured)` / `max(120, configured)`); the default ranges are unchanged for configurations within them, a panel edit still wins, and "Reset to config" restores both value and range. `TracerouteHops` was already consistent (configuration and panel both enforce 1–10).
2. **The GUI fell back to console mode for both entries (1.2.0 regression, P1).** Six control positions were written as `New-Object System.Drawing.Point(22, 84 + $offset)` (also `Size(900, 700 + $offset)` and `Point(22, $bottomY + 44)`). A `New-Object` argument list is parsed in expression mode, where the comma binds tighter than `+`, so `(22, 84 + $offset)` is the three-element array `22, 84, $offset` and the constructor threw "cannot find an overload for Point and the argument count 3". `Initialize-Gui` caught it, logged "The graphical interface could not be started. Console mode will be used.", and every double-click of `Start-NetworkCheck.cmd` or `Start-NetworkCheck-IT.cmd` ran in a console window. Introduced by v1.2.0 — up to 1.1.5 those lines were literals. Fix: the arithmetic is parenthesized.
3. **The IT launcher opened the user layout (1.2.0 regression, P1).** The top-level line `$script:Interactive = $false` ran after parameter binding and overwrote the bound `-Interactive` switch — a script's top-level scope and its `$script:` scope are the same variable table — so once the GUI could start at all, `Start-NetworkCheck-IT.cmd` showed the user window (no run-options panel, auto-start) with only `-ExpandDetails` in effect. Fix: the variable is initialized from the parameter and the redundant re-assignment in the entry block is removed.
4. **Validator** — two new guards per language file: no unparenthesized arithmetic inside a `New-Object` argument list, and no top-level `$script:<Parameter> = <literal>`; both flag the v1.2.0 files (six lines, and line 77 / 70) and pass on 1.2.1 — 62 → 66 checks. Version 1.2.1 in both scripts, READMEs, guides (§4.10 documents the panel-range rule, §7.2 the history); function count unchanged (77).

**Re-validation (Windows 11, Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100):**

| Step | Result |
|---|---|
| PS 5.1 parser, both languages | 0 errors × 2 |
| `validate_release.py` | 66 passed, 0 failed — skeleton identical, 77 = 77 functions, both new guards pass (and flag the v1.2.0 files when run against them), hashes regenerated (22 files) |
| Helper unit tests | 89 × 2 (unchanged) |
| Report-stage functional tests | 103 × 2 — new I: real WinForms panel controls created headless (no form): a configured 30 pings / 300 s is shown, the spinner ranges widen to 30 / 300, an untouched Start keeps 30 / 300 in the effective configuration and the profile text; a panel edit (12 / 45) still wins; "Reset to config" restores 30 / 300; the default configuration keeps the 1–20 / 1–120 ranges and shows 4 / 8; a CLI `-PingCount 25` is shown, not clamped; traceroute hops keep 1–10 |
| Headless `Initialize-Gui` (the function body executed without showing the form, both entries × both languages) | before the fix: all four threw at `$overall.Location = New-Object System.Drawing.Point(22, 84 + $offset)`; after: user form 940×700, IT form 940×872 with the panel populated |
| **GUI, user entry — real window (UI Automation)** | en-US and zh-TW: the window opens, the run starts by itself, JSON `EntryPoint = User`, ping count 4, sample 8 s, Overall Healthy, 28 results; closed with the Close button, exit 0 |
| **GUI, IT entry — real window (UI Automation)**, configuration `PingCount: 30`, `RetransmissionSampleSeconds: 125` | en-US and zh-TW: the title carries "- IT", the window is the panel layout (872 px logical), the run does not start by itself; Start clicked with the panel untouched → JSON `EntryPoint = IT`, `PingCount = 30`, `SampleSeconds = 125`, gateway ping 30/30, Overall Healthy, 28 results; closed with the Close button, exit 0. Before the fixes the same launch fell back to console mode, and with fix 2 alone it opened the user layout (title without "- IT", 700 px, auto-start) — both also reproduced through `Start-NetworkCheck-IT.cmd` itself |
| Acceptance, en-US user entry (console) | exit 0, Overall Healthy, 28 results (6 IT), fingerprint `healthy`, ~12 s |
| Acceptance, en-US IT entry (console switches: `-PingCount 6 -SampleSeconds 6 -PingTarget 8.8.8.8 -TcpTarget 1.1.1.1:53 -TracerouteHops 4 -ExpandDetails`) | exit 0, 30 results, `EntryPoint = IT`, ping count 6, sample 6 s, 4 hops, extra targets present, ~8 s |
| Acceptance, zh-TW user entry (console) | exit 0, Overall Healthy, 28 results (6 IT), ~10 s |

Lesson: "reviewed, not exercised headlessly" was not enough for WinForms code — the 1.2.0 GUI never opened on a real machine during its six review rounds because every acceptance run used `-ConsoleOnly`. From 1.2.1 the chain includes the headless `Initialize-Gui` smoke test and a UI Automation run of both entries in both languages (backlog #16 — the driver scripts are committed under `tests/` since 2026-09-04). The panel's spinner values cannot be read back through UI Automation (WinForms `NumericUpDown` exposes no value pattern here); the JSON run options are the evidence instead.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #4, round 1 · 2026-09-03.** One P2 finding on commit ade80fd, accepted and fixed:

1. *The new parameter guard only matched column 1.* A top-level `try` / `if` block creates no PowerShell scope but its statements are indented, so an indented `$script:Interactive = $false` inside the program-entry `try` block — the very regression the guard exists for — would have passed. Fix: the guard matches `$script:<Parameter> = …` at any indentation (a function assigning a literal would clobber the bound parameter just the same, so it is flagged too). Self-tests: the v1.2.0 files still flag line 77 / 70, an indented literal inside the entry `try` and a literal inside a function are flagged, and 1.2.1 is clean.

Re-validation after round 1 (the scripts are unchanged since the UI Automation runs; only the validator and the manifest changed): parser 0 errors × 2; validator 66/66; unit tests 89 × 2; report-stage functional tests 103 × 2; console acceptance en-US user, en-US IT switches and zh-TW user, exit 0, Overall Healthy.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #4, round 2 · 2026-09-03.** One P2 finding on commit fab12bc, accepted and fixed:

1. *The parameter guard accepted any right-hand side that merely contained the parameter name.* `$script:Interactive = $false # $Interactive` and `$script:Interactive = $InteractiveBackup` both passed. Fix: the line is stripped of comments (`#` outside quotes, `<# ... #>`) and of the contents of single-quoted strings (nothing expands there), and the assigned expression must contain the complete token `$Name` or `${Name}` (word boundary, so `$NameBackup` does not count). The arithmetic guard ignores comments the same way. Self-tests: v1.2.0 line 77 / 70 still flagged; a commented, a single-quoted and a longer-name reference are flagged; `[bool]$Interactive`, `${Interactive}`, `$Interactive.IsPresent`, `"$ConfigPath"` and `'a' + $ConfigPath` pass; 1.2.1 is clean.

Re-validation after round 2 (scripts unchanged; validator and manifest only): parser 0 errors × 2; validator 66/66; unit tests 89 × 2; report-stage functional tests 103 × 2; console acceptance en-US user, en-US IT switches and zh-TW user, exit 0, Overall Healthy.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #4, round 3 · 2026-09-03.** One P2 finding on commit 1004156, accepted and fixed:

1. *The parameter guard compared names case-sensitively.* PowerShell variable names are not, so `$script:interactive = $false` would have passed. Fix: parameter names, the `$script:` prefix and the right-hand-side token are matched case-insensitively, the `${script:Name}` spelling is recognised, and `Set-Variable` / `New-Variable` targeting a parameter at script or global scope is flagged as well. Self-tests extended accordingly (`$script:interactive = $false`, `${script:Interactive} = $false` and `Set-Variable -Scope Script -Name Interactive` are flagged; `$Script:Interactive = [bool]$interactive` and `${script:Interactive} = ${Interactive}` pass); v1.2.0 line 77 / 70 still flagged; 1.2.1 clean.

Re-validation after round 3 (scripts unchanged; validator and manifest only): parser 0 errors × 2; validator 66/66; unit tests 89 × 2; report-stage functional tests 103 × 2; console acceptance en-US user, en-US IT switches and zh-TW user, exit 0, Overall Healthy.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #4, round 4 · 2026-09-03.** One P2 finding on commit e98eaba, accepted and fixed:

1. *`Set-Variable` / `New-Variable` without `-Scope` was not flagged.* At the top level the cmdlet operates in the script scope, so `Set-Variable -Name Interactive -Value $false` would have overwritten the parameter unnoticed. Fix: the guard now tracks whether a line is inside a function (every function in these files opens with `function Name {` and closes with a column-0 `}`); outside a function an unscoped or `-Scope Local` cmdlet call, and likewise an unscoped assignment `$Interactive = ...` or `$local:Interactive = ...`, counts as an overwrite, while inside a function those are locals and are ignored; `$script:` / `$global:` forms and explicit script / global / numeric scopes are flagged anywhere; a positional `-Name` is recognised; the script's own param block is skipped. Self-tests: 30 top-level and 7 function-local cases, including the finding's example; v1.2.0 line 77 / 70 still flagged; 1.2.1 clean.

Re-validation after round 4 (scripts unchanged; validator and manifest only): parser 0 errors × 2; validator 66/66; unit tests 89 × 2; report-stage functional tests 103 × 2; console acceptance en-US user, en-US IT switches and zh-TW user, exit 0, Overall Healthy.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #4, round 5 · 2026-09-03.** One P2 finding on commit 61ab9a0, accepted and fixed:

1. *The arithmetic guard matched `New-Object` case-sensitively.* Command names are not, so `new-object System.Drawing.Point(22, 84 + $offset)` would have passed. Fix: the command is matched case-insensitively; the guard also covers the `New-Object -TypeName T -ArgumentList (...)` form (the same precedence pitfall) and analyses the balanced argument group even when more code follows on the line. Self-tests extended (lower-case and upper-case spellings, `-ArgumentList` with and without parentheses, trailing code, a `-Property` hashtable, nested `[math]::Min(560 + $offset, ...)`); the six v1.2.0 lines still flagged; 1.2.1 clean.

**Independent review — Codex, PR #4, round 6 · 2026-09-03 (requested by the maintainer after the five-round limit).** One P2 finding on commit a8e2f1b, accepted and fixed:

1. *A type-constrained assignment escaped the parameter guard.* The regex required `$` right after the indentation, so `[bool]$script:Interactive = $false` would have passed. Fix: the assignment pattern accepts any number of `[type]` / `[Attribute()]` prefixes (`[bool]$script:Interactive = ...`, `[ValidateNotNull()][string[]]$PingTarget = ...`); the script's own param block stays excluded, and a typed local inside a function stays a local. Self-tests extended (typed script-scope and unscoped assignments at the top level are flagged, the same with the parameter's own token or inside a function pass); v1.2.0 line 77 / 70 still flagged; 1.2.1 clean.
